3. import { ConfigService } from "@/effect/config-service"
4. import { Flag } from "@opencode-ai/core/flag/flag"
5. import { Config as EffectConfig, Context, Option, Redacted } from "effect"
6. 
7. export type Credentials = {
8.   password?: string
9.   username?: string
10. }
11. 
12. export type DecodedCredentials = {
13.   readonly username: string
14.   readonly password: Redacted.Redacted
15. }
16. 
17. export class Config extends ConfigService.Service<Config>()("@opencode/ServerAuthConfig", {
18.   password: EffectConfig.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD").pipe(EffectConfig.option),
19.   username: EffectConfig.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME").pipe(EffectConfig.withDefault("opencode")),
20. }) {}
21. 
22. export type Info = Context.Service.Shape<typeof Config>
23. 
24. export function required(config: Info) {
25.   return Option.isSome(config.password) && config.password.value !== ""
26. }
27. 
28. export function authorized(credentials: DecodedCredentials, config: Info) {
29.   return (
30.     Option.isSome(config.password) &&
31.     credentials.username === config.username &&
32.     Redacted.value(credentials.password) === config.password.value
33.   )
34. }
35. 
36. export function header(credentials?: Credentials) {
37.   const password = credentials?.password ?? Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD
38.   if (!password) return undefined
39. 
40.   const username = credentials?.username ?? Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME ?? "opencode"
41.   return `Basic ${Buffer.from(`${username}:${password}`).toString("base64")}`
42. }
43. 
44. export function headers(credentials?: Credentials) {
45.   const authorization = header(credentials)
46.   if (!authorization) return undefined
47.   return { Authorization: authorization }
48. }
49.
```

1. import { ServerAuth } from "@/server/auth"
2. import { Effect, Encoding, Layer, Redacted } from "effect"

7. export {
8.   Authorization as ServerAuthorization,
9.   authorizationLayer as serverAuthorizationLayer,
10. } from "@opencode-ai/server/middleware/authorization"
11. 
12. const AUTH_TOKEN_QUERY = "auth_token"
13. const UNAUTHORIZED = 401
14. const WWW_AUTHENTICATE = 'Basic realm="Secure Area"'
15. 
16. // Avoid HttpApiSecurity alternatives here: Effect security middleware wraps the
17. // full handler, so a downstream failure can make the next auth alternative run
18. // and remap an authorized NotFound into Unauthorized.
19. export class Authorization extends HttpApiMiddleware.Service<Authorization>()(
20.   "@opencode/ExperimentalHttpApiAuthorization",
21.   {
22.     error: HttpApiError.UnauthorizedNoContent,
23.   },
24. ) {}
25. 
26. export class PtyConnectAuthorization extends HttpApiMiddleware.Service<PtyConnectAuthorization>()(
27.   "@opencode/ExperimentalHttpApiPtyConnectAuthorization",
28.   {
29.     error: HttpApiError.UnauthorizedNoContent,
30.   },
31. ) {}
32. 
33. function emptyCredential() {
34.   return {
35.     username: "",
36.     password: Redacted.make(""),
37.   }
38. }
39. 
40. function validateCredential<A, E, R>(
41.   effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,
42.   credential: ServerAuth.DecodedCredentials,
43.   config: ServerAuth.Info,
44. ) {
45.   return Effect.gen(function* () {
46.     if (!ServerAuth.required(config)) return yield* effect
47.     if (!ServerAuth.authorized(credential, config)) {
48.       yield* HttpEffect.appendPreResponseHandler((_request, response) =>
49.         Effect.succeed(HttpServerResponse.setHeader(response, "www-authenticate", WWW_AUTHENTICATE)),
50.       )
51.       return yield* new HttpApiError.Unauthorized({})
52.     }
53.     return yield* effect
54.   })
55. }
56. 
57. function decodeCredential(input: string) {
58.   return Effect.fromResult(Encoding.decodeBase64String(input)).pipe(
59.     Effect.match({
60.       onFailure: emptyCredential,
61.       onSuccess: (header) => {
62.         const separator = header.indexOf(":")
63.         if (separator === -1) return emptyCredential()
64.         return {
65.           username: header.slice(0, separator),
66.           password: Redacted.make(header.slice(separator + 1)),
67.         }
68.       },
69.     }),
70.   )
71. }
72. 
73. function credentialFromRequest(request: HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest) {
74.   return credentialFromURL(new URL(request.url, "http://localhost"), request)
75. }
76. 
77. function credentialFromURL(url: URL, request: HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest) {
78.   const token = url.searchParams.get(AUTH_TOKEN_QUERY)
79.   if (token) return decodeCredential(token)
80.   const match = /^Basic\s+(.+)$/i.exec(request.headers.authorization ?? "")
81.   if (match) return decodeCredential(match[1])
82.   return Effect.succeed(emptyCredential())
83. }
84. 
85. function validateRawCredential<A, E, R>(
86.   effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,
87.   credential: ServerAuth.DecodedCredentials,
88.   config: ServerAuth.Info,
89. ) {
90.   if (!ServerAuth.required(config)) return effect
91.   if (!ServerAuth.authorized(credential, config))
92.     return Effect.succeed(
93.       HttpServerResponse.empty({
94.         status: UNAUTHORIZED,
95.         headers: { "www-authenticate": WWW_AUTHENTICATE },
96.       }),
97.     )
98.   return effect
99. }
100. 
101. export const authorizationRouterMiddleware = HttpRouter.middleware()(
102.   Effect.gen(function* () {
103.     const config = yield* ServerAuth.Config
104.     if (!ServerAuth.required(config)) return (effect) => effect
105. 
106.     return (effect) =>
107.       Effect.gen(function* () {
108.         const request = yield* HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest
109.         const url = new URL(request.url, "http://localhost")
110.         if (isPublicUIPath(request.method, url.pathname)) return yield* effect
111.         return yield* credentialFromURL(url, request).pipe(
112.           Effect.flatMap((credential) => validateRawCredential(effect, credential, config)),
113.         )
114.       })
115.   }),
116. )
117. 
118. export const authorizationLayer = Layer.effect(
119.   Authorization,
120.   Effect.gen(function* () {
121.     const config = yield* ServerAuth.Config
122.     if (!ServerAuth.required(config)) return Authorization.of((effect) => effect)
123.     return Authorization.of((effect) =>
124.       Effect.gen(function* () {
125.         const request = yield* HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest
126.         return yield* credentialFromRequest(request).pipe(
127.           Effect.flatMap((credential) => validateCredential(effect, credential, config)),
128.         )
129.       }),
130.     )
131.   }),
132. )
133. 
134. export const ptyConnectAuthorizationLayer = Layer.effect(

137.     const config = yield* ServerAuth.Config
138.     if (!ServerAuth.required(config)) return PtyConnectAuthorization.of((effect) => effect)
139.     return PtyConnectAuthorization.of((effect) =>

141.         const request = yield* HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest

<details>
<summary>13 lines</summary>

```
1. // Static UI assets the browser fetches without app-managed credentials, e.g.
2. // the manifest link in <head>. These bypass auth so the page can install/render
3. // the manifest icons even when a server password is configured.
4. export const PUBLIC_UI_PATHS = new Set<string>([
5.   "/site.webmanifest",
6.   "/web-app-manifest-192x192.png",
7.   "/web-app-manifest-512x512.png",
8. ])
9. 
10. export function isPublicUIPath(method: string, pathname: string) {
11.   return method === "GET" && PUBLIC_UI_PATHS.has(pathname)
12. }
13.
```

1. import { Config, Context, Effect, Layer } from "effect"
2. 
3. type ConfigMap = Record<string, Config.Config<unknown>>
4. 
5. /**
6.  * The service shape inferred from an object of Effect `Config` definitions.
7.  */
8. export type Shape<Fields extends ConfigMap> = {
9.   readonly [Key in keyof Fields]: Config.Success<Fields[Key]>
10. }
11. 
12. /**
13.  * A Context service class with generated layers for config-backed services.
14.  */
15. export type ServiceClass<Self, Id extends string, Service> = Context.ServiceClass<Self, Id, Service> & {
16.   /** Provide already-parsed config, useful in tests. */
17.   readonly layer: (input: Service) => Layer.Layer<Self>
18.   /** Parse config once from the active Effect ConfigProvider and provide the service. */
19.   readonly defaultLayer: Layer.Layer<Self, Config.ConfigError>
20. }
21. 
22. /**
23.  * Create a Context service whose implementation is derived from Effect `Config`.
24.  *
25.  * This keeps Effect `Config` as the source of truth for env names, defaults, and
26.  * validation while generating a typed service plus convenient production/test
27.  * layers.
28.  *
29.  * ```ts
30.  * class ServerAuthConfig extends ConfigService.Service<ServerAuthConfig>()(
31.  *   "@opencode/ServerAuthConfig",
32.  *   {
33.  *     password: Config.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD").pipe(Config.option),
34.  *     username: Config.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME").pipe(Config.withDefault("opencode")),
35.  *   },
36.  * ) {}
37.  *
38.  * const live = ServerAuthConfig.defaultLayer
39.  * const test = ServerAuthConfig.layer({ password: Option.some("secret"), username: "kit" })
40.  * ```
41.  */
42. export const Service =
43.   <Self>() =>
44.   <const Id extends string, const Fields extends ConfigMap>(id: Id, fields: Fields) => {
45.     class ConfigTag extends Context.Service<Self, Shape<Fields>>()(id) {
46.       static layer(input: Shape<Fields>) {
47.         return Layer.succeed(this, this.of(input))
48.       }
49. 
50.       static get defaultLayer() {
51.         const tag = this
52.         return Layer.effect(
53.           tag,
54.           Effect.gen(function* () {
55.             const config = yield* Config.all(fields)
56.             // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-type-assertion -- Config.all preserves the field shape, but its conditional return type also supports iterable inputs.
57.             return tag.of(config as Shape<Fields>)
58.           }),
59.         )
60.       }
61.     }
62. 
63.     // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-type-assertion -- The generated class carries typed static helpers.
64.     return ConfigTag as ServiceClass<Self, Id, Shape<Fields>>
65.   }
66. 
67. export * as ConfigService from "./config-service"
68.
````

1. import { NodeHttpServer } from "@effect/platform-node"
2. import { Credential } from "@opencode-ai/core/credential"
3. import { PermissionSaved } from "@opencode-ai/core/permission/saved"
4. import { Context, Layer, Option } from "effect"
5. import * as Effect from "effect/Effect"
6. import { HttpRouter, HttpServer } from "effect/unstable/http"
7. import { createServer } from "node:http"
8. import { createRoutes } from "@opencode-ai/server/routes"
9. import { Commands } from "../commands"
10. import { Runtime } from "../../framework/runtime"
11. import { Daemon } from "../../services/daemon"
12. 
13. export default Runtime.handler(
14.   Commands.commands.serve,
15.   Effect.fn("cli.serve")(function* (input) {
16.     return yield* Effect.scoped(
17.       Effect.gen(function* () {
18.         const daemon = yield* Daemon.Service
19.         const address = yield* listen(input.hostname, input.port, yield* daemon.password())
20.         if (input.register) yield* daemon.register(address)
21.         console.log(`server listening on ${HttpServer.formatAddress(address)}`)
22.         return yield* Effect.never
23.       }),
24.     )
25.   }),
26. )
27. 
28. function listen(hostname: string, port: Option.Option<number>, password: string) {
29.   if (Option.isSome(port)) return bind(hostname, port.value, password)
30.   // Preserve the familiar default when available, but let the OS choose a free
31.   // port when another local server already owns 4096.
32.   return bind(hostname, 4096, password).pipe(Effect.catch(() => bind(hostname, 0, password)))
33. }
34. 
35. function bind(hostname: string, port: number, password: string) {
36.   return Layer.build(
37.     HttpRouter.serve(createRoutes(password), { disableListenLog: true, disableLogger: true }).pipe(
38.       Layer.provideMerge(NodeHttpServer.layer(() => createServer(), { port, host: hostname })),
39.       Layer.provide(Credential.defaultLayer),
40.       Layer.provide(PermissionSaved.defaultLayer),
41.     ),
42.   ).pipe(Effect.map((context) => Context.get(context, HttpServer.HttpServer).address))
43. }
44.
```

1. import { Global } from "@opencode-ai/core/global"

3. import { createOpencodeClient } from "@opencode-ai/sdk/v2/client"
4. import { ServerAuth } from "@opencode-ai/server/auth"
5. import { Context, Effect, FileSystem, Layer, Option, Schedule, Schema, Scope } from "effect"
6. import { HttpServer } from "effect/unstable/http"
7. import { randomBytes, randomUUID } from "crypto"
8. import { spawn } from "node:child_process"
9. import path from "path"
10. 
11. export interface Interface {
12.   readonly client: () => Effect.Effect<ReturnType<typeof createOpencodeClient>, unknown>
13.   readonly transport: () => Effect.Effect<{ url: string; headers: RequestInit["headers"] }, unknown>
14.   readonly start: () => Effect.Effect<string, Error>
15.   readonly status: () => Effect.Effect<string | undefined>
16.   readonly stop: () => Effect.Effect<void, unknown>
17.   readonly password: (value?: string) => Effect.Effect<string, unknown>
18.   readonly register: (address: HttpServer.Address) => Effect.Effect<void, unknown, Scope.Scope>
19. }
20. 
21. export class Service extends Context.Service<Service, Interface>()("@opencode/cli/Daemon") {}
22. 
23. const Registration = Schema.Struct({
24.   id: Schema.optional(Schema.String),
25.   version: Schema.optional(Schema.String),
26.   url: Schema.String,
27.   pid: Schema.Int.check(Schema.isGreaterThan(0)),
28. })
29. type Registration = typeof Registration.Type
30. 
31. function sameRegistration(left: Registration, right: Registration) {
32.   return left.id === right.id && left.version === right.version && left.url === right.url && left.pid === right.pid
33. }
34. 
35. export const layer = Layer.effect(
36.   Service,
37.   Effect.gen(function* () {
38.     const fs = yield* FileSystem.FileSystem
39.     const directory = Global.Path.state
40.     const file = path.join(directory, "server.json")
41.     const passwordFile = path.join(directory, "password")
42.     const decodeRegistration = Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(Schema.fromJsonString(Registration))
43. 
44.     const password = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.password")(function* (value?: string) {
45.       const existing = yield* fs.readFileString(passwordFile).pipe(Effect.catch(() => Effect.succeed(undefined)))
46.       if (value === undefined && existing) return existing
47. 
48.       // Keep one private credential across server restarts so discovered clients
49.       // can reconnect without exposing a password flag or environment variable.
50.       const generated = value ?? randomBytes(32).toString("base64url")
51.       const temp = passwordFile + ".tmp"
52.       yield* fs.makeDirectory(directory, { recursive: true })
53.       yield* fs.writeFileString(temp, generated, { mode: 0o600 })
54.       yield* fs.rename(temp, passwordFile)
55.       return generated
56.     })
57. 
58.     const registration = Effect.fnUntraced(function* () {
59.       return yield* fs.readFileString(file).pipe(Effect.flatMap(decodeRegistration))
60.     })
61. 
62.     const createClient = Effect.fnUntraced(function* (url: string) {
63.       return createOpencodeClient({ baseUrl: url, headers: ServerAuth.headers({ password: yield* password() }) })
64.     })
65. 
66.     const healthy = Effect.fnUntraced(function* () {
67.       const info = yield* registration()
68.       const client = yield* createClient(info.url)
69.       const response = yield* Effect.tryPromise(() => client.v2.health.get({ signal: AbortSignal.timeout(2_000) }))
70.       if (response.data?.healthy === true) return info
71.       return yield* Effect.fail(new Error("Registered server is not healthy"))
72.     })
73. 
74.     const compatible = Effect.fnUntraced(function* () {
75.       const info = yield* healthy()
76.       if (info.version === InstallationVersion) return info
77.       return yield* Effect.fail(new Error("Registered server version does not match the client"))
78.     })
79. 
80.     const signal = (pid: number, signal: NodeJS.Signals) =>
81.       Effect.try({ try: () => process.kill(pid, signal), catch: (cause) => cause }).pipe(Effect.ignore)
82. 
83.     const awaitStopped = Effect.fnUntraced(function* (pid: number) {
84.       const running = yield* Effect.try({ try: () => process.kill(pid, 0), catch: () => false }).pipe(
85.         Effect.orElseSucceed(() => false),
86.       )
87.       if (!running) return true
88.       return yield* Effect.fail(new Error(`Server process ${pid} is still running`))
89.     })
90. 
91.     const stopProcess = Effect.fnUntraced(function* (info: Registration) {
92.       const current = yield* healthy().pipe(Effect.option)
93.       if (Option.isNone(current) || !sameRegistration(current.value, info)) return
94. 
95.       yield* signal(info.pid, "SIGTERM")
96.       const stopped = yield* awaitStopped(info.pid).pipe(
97.         Effect.retry(Schedule.spaced("50 millis").pipe(Schedule.both(Schedule.recurs(100)))),
98.         Effect.option,
99.       )
100.       if (Option.isSome(stopped)) return
101. 
102.       const latest = yield* healthy().pipe(Effect.option)
103.       if (Option.isNone(latest) || !sameRegistration(latest.value, info)) return
104.       yield* signal(info.pid, "SIGKILL")
105.       yield* awaitStopped(info.pid).pipe(
106.         Effect.retry(Schedule.spaced("50 millis").pipe(Schedule.both(Schedule.recurs(100)))),
107.       )
108.     })
109. 
110.     const start = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.start")(function* () {
111.       const existing = yield* healthy().pipe(Effect.option)
112.       const found = Option.getOrUndefined(existing)
113.       const compiled = path.basename(process.execPath).replace(/\.exe$/, "") !== "bun"
114.       if (found?.version === InstallationVersion && compiled) return found.url
115.       if (found) yield* stopProcess(found).pipe(Effect.ignore)
116. 
117.       const entrypoint = compiled ? undefined : process.argv[1]
118.       if (!compiled && entrypoint === undefined)
119.         return yield* Effect.fail(new Error("Failed to resolve CLI entrypoint"))
120.       yield* Effect.try({
121.         try: () => {
122.           spawn(process.execPath, [...(entrypoint ? [entrypoint] : []), "serve", "--register"], {
123.             detached: true,
124.             stdio: "ignore",
125.           }).unref()
126.         },
127.         catch: (cause) => new Error("Failed to start server", { cause }),
128.       })
129. 
130.       return yield* compatible().pipe(
131.         Effect.retry(Schedule.spaced("50 millis").pipe(Schedule.both(Schedule.recurs(100)))),
132.         Effect.map((info) => info.url),
133.         Effect.mapError(() => new Error("Failed to start server")),
134.       )
135.     })
136. 
137.     const transport = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.transport")(function* () {
138.       return { url: yield* start(), headers: ServerAuth.headers({ password: yield* password() }) }
139.     })
140. 
141.     const client = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.client")(function* () {
142.       const connection = yield* transport()
143.       return createOpencodeClient({ baseUrl: connection.url, headers: connection.headers })
144.     })
145. 
146.     const status = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.status")(function* () {
147.       const existing = yield* healthy().pipe(Effect.option)
148.       const found = Option.getOrUndefined(existing)
149.       if (found?.version === InstallationVersion) return found.url
150.       if (found) return undefined
151.       yield* fs.remove(file).pipe(Effect.ignore)
152.       return undefined
153.     })
154. 
155.     const stop = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.stop")(function* () {
156.       const existing = yield* healthy().pipe(Effect.option)
157.       // A stale registration may point at a PID that has since been reused by
158.       // another process. Only signal the PID after authenticating the server.
159.       if (Option.isNone(existing)) return yield* fs.remove(file).pipe(Effect.ignore)
160.       yield* stopProcess(existing.value)
161.       yield* fs.remove(file).pipe(Effect.ignore)
162.     })
163. 
164.     const register = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.register")(function* (address: HttpServer.Address) {
165.       const id = randomUUID()
166.       const temp = file + "." + id + ".tmp"
167.       yield* fs.makeDirectory(directory, { recursive: true })
168.       yield* fs.writeFileString(
169.         temp,
170.         JSON.stringify({ id, version: InstallationVersion, url: HttpServer.formatAddress(address), pid: process.pid }),
171.         { mode: 0o600 },
172.       )
173.       yield* fs.rename(temp, file)
174.       yield* registration().pipe(
175.         Effect.flatMap((info) => (info.id === id ? Effect.void : signal(process.pid, "SIGTERM"))),
176.         Effect.catch(() => signal(process.pid, "SIGTERM")),
177.         Effect.repeat(Schedule.spaced("10 seconds")),
178.         Effect.forkScoped,
179.       )
180.       yield* Effect.addFinalizer(() =>
181.         registration().pipe(
182.           Effect.flatMap((info) => (info.id === id ? fs.remove(file) : Effect.void)),
183.           Effect.ignore,
184.         ),
185.       )
186.     })
187. 
188.     return Service.of({ client, transport, start, status, stop, password, register })
189.   }),
190. )
191. 
192. export const defaultLayer = layer
193. 
194. export * as Daemon from "./daemon"
195.
```

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I understand the issue. Looking at the `daemon.ts` code:

```ts
const password = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.password")(function* (value?: string) {
  const existing = yield* fs.readFileString(passwordFile).pipe(Effect.catch(() =\> Effect.succeed(undefined)))
  if (value === undefined && existing) return existing

  // Keep one private credential across server restarts so discovered clients
  // can reconnect without exposing a password flag or environment variable.
  const generated = value ?? randomBytes(32).toString("base64url")
  const temp = passwordFile + ".tmp"


```

The function creates the directory if needed, writes the generated password to a temporary file with restricted permissions, then atomically renames it to the final location. Over in `serve.ts`, the daemon password is passed to the listen function regardless of whether an environment variable was set, so the server always uses this persisted credential.

Now I'm looking at how the password gets passed into the route creation...*

<sub>⏱️ 1m 6s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see the full picture. 

In `packages/cli/src/commands/handlers/serve.ts`:
```ts
const address = yield* listen(input.hostname, input.port, yield* daemon.password())
```

The `daemon.password()` method (in daemon.ts) checks if a password file already exists and if so returns it, otherwise generates a new random password and saves it. This means the server ALWAYS has a password.

Then in `routes.ts`:
```ts
password
  ? ServerAuth.Config.layer({ username: "opencode", password: Option.some(password) })
  : ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer,
```

If `password` is provided (which it always will be from `daemon.password()`), it sets auth as required. The user's issue is: when running `opencode serve --hostname 0.0.0.0 --port 4096 --mdns`, the server asks for a password even though they didn't set `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`. The design rationale in the code comment explains this is the "daemon" mode password - it's always generated and persisted across server restarts so discovered clients can reconnect without needing to expose a password flag or environment variable.

The tension here is that when users explicitly run `opencode serve`, they might expect no password requirement, but the daemon mode always generates one for security. I need to check what `--mdns` actually does in the CLI arguments and whether it's related to the `--register` flag in the code.*

<sub>⏱️ 1m 17s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I understand the full picture. Here's the problem:

1. When `opencode serve` runs, it calls `daemon.password()` which:
   - Reads the password from `~/.local/state/opencode/password` (or wherever the state dir is)
   - If the file exists (from a previous run), it returns that password
   - If no file exists, it generates a random password and saves it

2. This password is ALWAYS set when running via the CLI (not `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` env var, but a file-based password that is auto-generated).

3. Even without setting `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, the user will always be prompted for a password because the daemon always generates/uses one.
   
The real issue is that the `serve` handler unconditionally calls `daemon.password()`, which means a password is always generated and enforced. For local or trusted network scenarios, the user should be able to disable this password requirement entirely, but right now there's no way to opt out of the auto-generated password mechanism. The real distinction here is between running as a background daemon versus an explicit server. When the daemon registers itself in the background, it needs that auto-generated password for security. But if someone explicitly runs `opencode serve` on a trusted network without setting `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, they shouldn't be forced into authentication. I should check whether there's a `--register` flag to differentiate these cases.*

---

<sub>⏱️ 1m 32s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see the full picture. Looking at the `serve.ts` in `packages/cli/`:

```ts
const address = yield* listen(input.hostname, input.port, yield* daemon.password())
```

And in `daemon.ts`:

```ts
const password = Effect.fn("cli.daemon.password")(function* (value?: string) {
  const existing = yield* fs.readFileString(passwordFile).pipe(Effect.catch(() =\> Effect.succeed(undefined)))
  if (value === undefined && existing) return existing

  // Keep one private credential across server restarts so discovered clients


```

The daemon always generates or reuses a random password stored in a file, regardless of any environment variable. But there's also `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` in `packages/server/src/routes.ts` that reads `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, which gets used when the password argument is undefined. So the real issue is that `serve.ts` always invokes `daemon.password()`, which means it's always generating its own credential instead of respecting the environment variable path.

The flow is: the daemon generates a random password from the local state file, passes it to `createRoutes`, which then creates auth config with that password always enabled. This means password protection is forced on even when the user didn't set `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and expects the server to run without authentication.

Looking at how `serve.ts` sets this up...

```

ts
const address = yield* listen(input.hostname, input.port, yield* daemon.password())
```

I see now—the design intentionally auto-generates a password when running as a daemon for security, so random clients can't connect. But the user is explicitly running `opencode serve` with specific flags and wants to control whether authentication is required via the environment variable. The password file approach was meant for the daemon spawned by the CLI, not for when someone directly invokes the serve command and expects `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` to dictate the auth behavior.

I think the real fix is to check whether the password file actually exists before using it, or respect the environment variable when it's explicitly set—maybe add a `--no-password` flag as an option, or just skip password generation if the env var isn't configured.

Let me check how the serve command is structured to see what flags are available.*

110.     }
111.   },
112. )
113. 
114. function listenerLayer(opts: ListenOptions, port: number) {
115.   return HttpRouter.serve(HttpApiApp.createRoutes(opts), {
116.     middleware: disposeMiddleware,
117.     disableLogger: true,
118.     disableListenLog: true,
119.   }).pipe(
120.     Layer.provideMerge(WebSocketTracker.layer),
121.     Layer.provideMerge(serverLayer({ port, hostname: opts.hostname })),
122.     // Install a fresh `ConfigProvider` per listener so `Config.string(...)`
123.     // reads reflect the current `process.env`. Effect's default
124.     // `ConfigProvider` snapshots `process.env` on first read and caches the
125.     // result on a module-singleton Reference; without overriding it here,
126.     // every later `Server.listen()` keeps observing that initial snapshot.
127.     Layer.provide(ConfigProvider.layer(ConfigProvider.fromEnv())),
128.   )
129. }
130. 
131. function startWithPortFallback(opts: ListenOptions) {
132.   if (opts.port !== 0) return startListener(opts, opts.port)
133.   // Match the legacy listener port-resolution behavior: explicit `0` prefers
134.   // 4096 first, then any free port.
135.   return startListener(opts, 4096).pipe(Effect.catch(() => startListener(opts, 0)))
136. }
137. 
138. function startListener(opts: ListenOptions, port: number) {
139.   const scope = Scope.makeUnsafe()
140.   return Layer.buildWithMemoMap(listenerLayer(opts, port), Layer.makeMemoMapUnsafe(), scope).pipe(
141.     Effect.provide(HttpApiApp.context),
142.     Effect.onError(() => Scope.close(scope, Exit.void).pipe(Effect.ignore)),
143.     Effect.map(
144.       (ctx): ListenerState => ({
145.         scope,
146.         server: Context.get(ctx, HttpServer.HttpServer),
147.         http: Context.get(ctx, ListenerServerService),
148.         websockets: Context.get(ctx, WebSocketTracker.Service),
149.       }),
150.     ),
151.   )
152. }
153. 
154. function tcpAddress(state: ListenerState) {
155.   return Effect.gen(function* () {
156.     if (state.server.address._tag === "TcpAddress") return state.server.address
157.     yield* Scope.close(state.scope, Exit.void).pipe(Effect.ignore)
158.     return yield* Effect.die(new Error(`Unexpected HttpServer address tag: ${state.server.address._tag}`))
159.   })
160. }
161. 
162. function makeURL(hostname: string, port: number) {
163.   const result = new URL("http://localhost")
164.   result.hostname = hostname
165.   result.port = String(port)
166.   return result
167. }
168. 
169. function setupMdns(opts: ListenOptions, port: number, scope: Scope.Scope) {
170.   return Effect.gen(function* () {
171.     const publish =
172.       opts.mdns && port && opts.hostname !== "127.0.0.1" && opts.hostname !== "localhost" && opts.hostname !== "::1"
173.     if (publish) {
174.       const unpublish = yield* Effect.cached(Effect.sync(() => MDNS.unpublish()))
175.       yield* Effect.sync(() => MDNS.publish(port, opts.mdnsDomain))
176.       yield* Scope.addFinalizer(scope, unpublish)
177.       return unpublish
178.     }
179.     if (opts.mdns) {
180.       yield* Effect.logWarning("mDNS enabled but hostname is loopback; skipping mDNS publish")
181.     }
182.     return Effect.void
183.   })
184. }
185. 
186. function makeStop(state: ListenerState, unpublishMdns: Effect.Effect<void>, listenerUrl: URL) {
187.   return Effect.gen(function* () {
188.     const forceCloseOnce = yield* Effect.cached(forceClose(state).pipe(Effect.ignore))
189.     const closeScopeOnce = yield* Effect.cached(
190.       Scope.close(state.scope, Exit.void).pipe(
191.         Effect.ignore,
192.         Effect.ensuring(
193.           Effect.sync(() => {
194.             if (url === listenerUrl) url = undefined
195.           }),
196.         ),
197.       ),
198.     )
199. 
200.     return (close?: boolean) =>
201.       Effect.gen(function* () {
202.         yield* unpublishMdns
203.         if (close) yield* forceCloseOnce
204.         yield* closeScopeOnce
205.       })
206.   })
207. }
208. 
209. function forceClose(state: ListenerState) {
210.   return Effect.all([state.http.closeAll, state.websockets.closeAll], { concurrency: "unbounded", discard: true })
211. }
212. 
213. function serverLayer(opts: { port: number; hostname: string }) {
214.   const server = createServer()
215.   const serverRef = { closeStarted: false, forceStop: false }
216.   const close = server.close.bind(server)
217.   // Keep shutdown owned by NodeHttpServer, but honor listener.stop(true) by
218.   // force-closing active HTTP sockets when its finalizer calls server.close().
219.   // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-type-assertion -- Node's overloads don't preserve a monkey-patched method assignment.
220.   server.close = ((callback?: Parameters<typeof server.close>[0]) => {
221.     serverRef.closeStarted = true
222.     const result = close(callback)
223.     if (serverRef.forceStop) server.closeAllConnections()
224.     return result
225.   }) as typeof server.close
226. 
227.   return Layer.mergeAll(
228.     NodeHttpServer.layer(() => server, { port: opts.port, host: opts.hostname, gracefulShutdownTimeout: "1 second" }),
229.     Layer.succeed(ListenerServerService)(
230.       ListenerServerService.of({
231.         closeAll: Effect.sync(() => {
232.           serverRef.forceStop = true
233.           if (serverRef.closeStarted) server.closeAllConnections()
234.         }),
235.       }),
236.     ),
237.   )
238. }
239. 
240. export * as Server from "./server"
241.
```

41.   const { Config } = yield* Effect.promise(() => import("@/config/config"))

46. export function resolveNetworkOptionsNoConfig(args: NetworkOptions, config?: ConfigV1.Info) {
47.   const portExplicitlySet = process.argv.includes("--port")
48.   const hostnameExplicitlySet = process.argv.includes("--hostname")
49.   const mdnsExplicitlySet = process.argv.includes("--mdns")
50.   const mdnsDomainExplicitlySet = process.argv.includes("--mdns-domain")
51.   const mdns = mdnsExplicitlySet ? args.mdns : (config?.server?.mdns ?? args.mdns)
52.   const mdnsDomain = mdnsDomainExplicitlySet ? args["mdns-domain"] : (config?.server?.mdnsDomain ?? args["mdns-domain"])
53.   const port = portExplicitlySet ? args.port : (config?.server?.port ?? args.port)
54.   const hostname = hostnameExplicitlySet
55.     ? args.hostname
56.     : mdns && !config?.server?.hostname
57.       ? "0.0.0.0"
58.       : (config?.server?.hostname ?? args.hostname)
59.   const configCors = config?.server?.cors ?? []
60.   const argsCors = Array.isArray(args.cors) ? args.cors : args.cors ? [args.cors] : []
61.   const cors = [...configCors, ...argsCors]
62. 
63.   return { hostname, port, mdns, mdnsDomain, cors }

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see the full picture. Looking at `server.ts`:

```ts
const authOnlyRouterLayer = authorizationRouterMiddleware.layer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
const httpApiAuthLayer = authorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
const ptyConnectHttpApiAuthLayer = ptyConnectAuthorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
const serverHttpApiAuthLayer = serverAuthorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
```

These all use `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`, which reads `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` from the environment. 

If the user doesn't set that environment variable, password auth should be disabled since the config uses `Config.option`. But they're saying it's still asking for a password, which doesn't add up. Let me check what's happening in the opencode package's `auth.ts` file.*

### ✅ `view`

**view the file at /Users/engineer/workspace/opencode/packages/opencode/src/server/auth.ts.**

/Users/engineer/workspace/opencode/packages/opencode/src/server/auth.ts

<details>
<summary>49 lines</summary>

```
1. export * as ServerAuth from "./auth"
2. 
3. import { ConfigService } from "@/effect/config-service"
4. import { Flag } from "@opencode-ai/core/flag/flag"
5. import { Config as EffectConfig, Context, Option, Redacted } from "effect"
6. 
7. export type Credentials = {
8.   password?: string
9.   username?: string
10. }
11. 
12. export type DecodedCredentials = {
13.   readonly username: string
14.   readonly password: Redacted.Redacted
15. }
16. 
17. export class Config extends ConfigService.Service<Config>()("@opencode/ServerAuthConfig", {
18.   password: EffectConfig.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD").pipe(EffectConfig.option),
19.   username: EffectConfig.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME").pipe(EffectConfig.withDefault("opencode")),
20. }) {}
21. 
22. export type Info = Context.Service.Shape<typeof Config>
23. 
24. export function required(config: Info) {
25.   return Option.isSome(config.password) && config.password.value !== ""
26. }
27. 
28. export function authorized(credentials: DecodedCredentials, config: Info) {
29.   return (
30.     Option.isSome(config.password) &&
31.     credentials.username === config.username &&

### 💭 Reasoning

*OK! Now I see it. The `serve.ts` in the opencode package correctly uses `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` to warn when no password is set. The `Server.listen()` uses `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` which reads from `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`.

But the `server.ts` in `packages/opencode/src/server/` has these auth layers defined at module level (not per-request):

```ts
const authOnlyRouterLayer = authorizationRouterMiddleware.layer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
const httpApiAuthLayer = authorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))


```

And there's a comment in `listenerLayer` explaining that a fresh `ConfigProvider` is installed per listener so that `Config.string(...)` reads reflect the current `process.env`, since Effect's default provider snapshots environment variables on first read and caches them. But here's the problem: the auth layers are defined at module level, not inside `listenerLayer`, so they're capturing the environment snapshot from when the module first loads rather than when each listener starts.

Actually, wait—Effect's `Layer` compositions are lazy, so `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` should pick up the fresh `ConfigProvider` at build time. But the auth layers are still composed at module level, so I need to trace through exactly when they get built and whether they see the fresh provider that's supplied per-listener. The Effect layer system is lazy and compositional, so the ConfigProvider should flow down through the entire layer tree when everything gets built together. Let me trace through how `listenerLayer` composes things — it's serving routes created by `HttpApiApp.createRoutes`, then merging in the WebSocket tracker and server layer, and finally providing the ConfigProvider from environment variables.

The `createRoutes` function merges together module-level constants like `rootApiRoutes` and `eventApiRoutes`, which themselves include the authorization layer with its default config. Since the ConfigProvider is provided at the `listenerLayer` level, it should be available to all the nested sub-layers when they're resolved. The question is whether `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` can actually access that ConfigProvider from the context when it needs it.

Let me look back at the actual problem — the user is running the serve command and getting a password prompt, which suggests the auth configuration might not be reading the environment variables correctly. Looking at the serve.ts file, there's a check for a password flag that seems to be triggering the prompt. I need to trace through how the `opencode serve` command reads the password configuration, specifically looking at how `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is being handled and whether the auth layer is correctly checking if a password is actually required.*

<sub>⏱️ 3m 26s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*`Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is read at module load time (`process.env["OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD"]`), not as a getter. This means it captures the value at module initialization.

But for auth in the server, `ServerAuth.Config` uses Effect's `Config.string("OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD").pipe(Config.option)`. The key insight is in `server.ts`:

```ts
// Install a fresh `ConfigProvider` per listener so `Config.string(...)`
// reads reflect the current `process.env`. Effect's default
// `ConfigProvider` snapshots `process.env` on first read and caches the


// result on a module-singleton Reference; without overriding it here,
// every later `Server.listen()` keeps observing that initial snapshot.
Layer.provide(ConfigProvider.layer(ConfigProvider.fromEnv())),
```

So there IS a fresh `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` added per listener. This should make `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` correctly pick up `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` from the environment when not set.

But wait — looking at `server.ts` more carefully, I see the auth router layer is being provided with `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`, and I need to trace through whether that's actually getting the fresh config provider or if there's a disconnect in how the layers are being composed.

Examining the module-level constants that set up the auth layers for different routes — they all provide `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` at definition time. The question is whether these module-level layers can access the `ConfigProvider` that gets injected later when the listener layer is created, or if they've already resolved their dependencies independently.

Looking at how `defaultLayer` works, it uses `Config.all(fields)` which reads from the ambient `ConfigProvider` in the context. When `Layer.provide` composes layers, it passes the `ConfigProvider` service through the context, so the auth layers should pick up whatever `ConfigProvider` is available when they're actually built — not when they're defined at the module level.

Now I'm tracing through the actual layer composition. The `listenerLayer` has `ConfigProvider.layer(ConfigProvider.fromEnv())` at the bottom, which means all the sub-layers including the auth ones should be using that fresh `ConfigProvider` that reads from environment variables. If `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't set in the environment, `Config.all(fields)` should return `None` and auth shouldn't be required. But the user is still seeing a password prompt, so either the environment variable is actually set somewhere (maybe in their shell or dotfiles from a previous session), or there's something else triggering the Basic auth dialog in the browser. Let me check if there's a password field being set elsewhere in the config. Both commands warn when the password isn't set, which suggests the server should work without authentication in that case. But the user is being prompted for a password anyway, so maybe there's a default password being set somewhere in the config or initialization logic that I'm missing.

Looking at how the routes are constructed, `createRoutes` returns a layer that references module-level constants like `rootApiRoutes` and `eventApiRoutes`. These constants define the auth layers, but in Effect's layer system, even though they're defined at the module level, they get re-evaluated fresh each time the layer is composed...

Actually, I need to verify something about how `ConfigProvider` propagates through the layer tree. When `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` is included as a dependency within `listenerLayer`, and `listenerLayer` provides a fresh `ConfigProvider` from environment variables, that config should be available to all sub-layers in the composition, including the auth layer.

But I'm noticing there's a `memoMap` being used in the web handler setup, which could be caching layer instances. ### 💭 Reasoning

*OK, so there's a `memoMap` that's shared across the whole app. And looking at `startListener`:```ts
function startListener(opts: ListenOptions, port: number) {
  const scope = Scope.makeUnsafe()
  return Layer.buildWithMemoMap(listenerLayer(opts, port), Layer.makeMemoMapUnsafe(), scope).pipe(
```

It uses `Layer.makeMemoMapUnsafe()` for building the listener, so each listener has its own memoMap. That's good.But wait - `listenerLayer` includes `createRoutes(opts)` from `HttpApiApp.createRoutes`. And `createRoutes` uses module-level constants like `rootApiRoutes` which use `httpApiAuthLayer = authorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))`.


So the `httpApiAuthLayer` is defined at the module level with `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`, which reads from environment config like `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` wrapped in an `Option`.

When `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` is used and the env var isn't set, the missing key error gets caught by `Config.option` and returns `None`. This means `config.password` would be `Option.none()`, making `ServerAuth.required()` return `false` and skipping auth entirely.

But there might be a `~/.opencode/config.json` file that's setting a server password instead. Let me check the `ConfigServerV1` schema to see if it supports a `server.password` field...

Examining the schema definition...

No `password` field exists in the server config, so it can't be coming from there. Now I'm wondering if the `opencode.json` config affects the `ConfigProvider` itself. The `server.ts` file creates a fresh `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` for each listener and provides it via a layer, which overrides the default Effect ConfigProvider that reads from `process.env`. But looking more closely at how the module-level auth is set up...

The key question is whether this fresh ConfigProvider actually flows into the auth layers when `listenerLayer` is composed. In Effect, layer composition order matters—when you provide one layer with another, the dependencies get satisfied in that order. So I need to trace through how `Layer.provide` and `Layer.provideMerge` work together in the `listenerLayer` function to see if the new ConfigProvider reaches the auth layer constants.

Actually, let me step back and focus on what the user is actually experiencing. They're getting a password prompt despite not setting `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, so there are a few simpler possibilities: maybe a config file is setting a password, or there's a bug in the authorization middleware that always rejects requests, or the www-authenticate header is being set incorrectly, or an old process is still running with different configuration.

Looking at the authorization middleware, if no password is configured, it should just pass the request through without requiring authentication. Let me trace through the actual layer setup to see where the issue might be.

Wait, I think I found it — the user might be running the CLI from `packages/cli` rather than the main `packages/opencode` package. The CLI's serve command always calls `daemon.password()`, which generates or reads a password from a file regardless of whether one was explicitly set. Let me check what that function does.

The password function ensures a password always exists — it either retrieves an existing one from storage or generates a new one if needed. So the `packages/cli` serve command will always have a password enabled.

Now the real question is which binary the user is actually running. The `--mdns` flag they're using is defined in `packages/opencode`'s CLI, so that's a clue about which one it might be.

Since they're running the `packages/opencode` CLI, auth should be controlled by the `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` environment variable. The user says they didn't set it, so the server shouldn't require authentication — but it does. I'm wondering if there's a `config.json` or `opencode.json` file somewhere that's setting a server password, or if the `ConfigProvider` is somehow injecting `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` from the config.

Actually, maybe there's a `.env` file in their working directory with the password set. Or I should look more carefully at whether the server is sending the `www-authenticate` header even when auth isn't required. Let me check the credential validation logic to see how it determines whether authentication is actually needed.

The validation function checks if `ServerAuth.required(config)` is false, meaning no password is configured, and if so it just runs the effect without authentication. The `required` function itself checks whether the password config is `Some` and non-empty. So the logic looks sound — if `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't set, the config should reflect that.

But I'm realizing I need to understand exactly how `Config.option` behaves when an environment variable is missing versus when it's set to an empty string. If the env var is unset, it returns `None`. If it's set to an empty string, `Config.string` would succeed with `""`, and then `Config.option` would wrap that in `Some("")`. The `required` check would then catch this and return `false` since it explicitly checks for non-empty values.

So even with an empty string, authentication shouldn't be triggered. I'm wondering if the issue is actually coming from somewhere else—maybe the browser is caching credentials, or perhaps there's middleware or another handler returning a 401 status that's triggering the browser's built-in password prompt. Let me check if there are other places in the codebase setting the `www-authenticate` header or returning 401 responses, or if the login page itself has something that's prompting for a password.

Actually, I should look at the server connection utilities—there might be password validation happening on the client side when checking the server's health or status.*

1. import { createOpencodeClient } from "@opencode-ai/sdk/v2/client"
2. import type { ServerConnection } from "@/context/server"
3. import { decode64 } from "@/utils/base64"
4. 
5. export function authTokenFromCredentials(input: { username?: string; password: string }) {
6.   return btoa(`${input.username ?? "opencode"}:${input.password}`)
7. }
8. 
9. export function authFromToken(token: string | null) {
10.   const decoded = decode64(token ?? undefined)
11.   if (!decoded) return
12.   const separator = decoded.indexOf(":")
13.   if (separator === -1) return
14.   return {
15.     username: decoded.slice(0, separator) || "opencode",
16.     password: decoded.slice(separator + 1),
17.   }
18. }
19. 
20. export function createSdkForServer({
21.   server,
22.   ...config
23. }: Omit<NonNullable<Parameters<typeof createOpencodeClient>[0]>, "baseUrl"> & {
24.   server: ServerConnection.HttpBase
25. }) {
26.   const auth = (() => {
27.     if (!server.password) return
28.     return {
29.       Authorization: `Basic ${authTokenFromCredentials({ username: server.username, password: server.password })}`,
30.     }
31.   })()
32. 
33.   return createOpencodeClient({
34.     ...config,
35.     headers: {
36.       ...(config.headers instanceof Headers ? Object.fromEntries(config.headers.entries()) : config.headers),
37.       ...auth,
38.     },
39.     baseUrl: server.url,
40.   })
41. }
42.

1. import { usePlatform } from "@/context/platform"
2. import { ServerConnection } from "@/context/server"
3. import { createSdkForServer } from "./server"
4. import { Accessor, createEffect, onCleanup } from "solid-js"
5. import { createStore, reconcile } from "solid-js/store"
6. 
7. export type ServerHealth = { healthy: boolean; version?: string }
8. 
9. interface CheckServerHealthOptions {
10.   timeoutMs?: number
11.   signal?: AbortSignal
12.   retryCount?: number
13.   retryDelayMs?: number
14. }
15. 
16. const defaultTimeoutMs = 30_000
17. const defaultRetryCount = 2
18. const defaultRetryDelayMs = 100
19. const cacheMs = 750
20. const healthCache = new Map<
21.   string,
22.   { at: number; done: boolean; fetch: typeof globalThis.fetch; promise: Promise<ServerHealth> }
23. >()
24. 
25. function cacheKey(server: ServerConnection.HttpBase) {
26.   return `${server.url}\n${server.username ?? ""}\n${server.password ?? ""}`
27. }
28. 
29. function timeoutSignal(timeoutMs: number) {
30.   const timeout = (AbortSignal as unknown as { timeout?: (ms: number) => AbortSignal }).timeout
31.   if (timeout) {
32.     try {
33.       return {
34.         signal: timeout.call(AbortSignal, timeoutMs),
35.         clear: undefined as (() => void) | undefined,
36.       }
37.     } catch {}
38.   }
39.   const controller = new AbortController()
40.   const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeoutMs)
41.   return { signal: controller.signal, clear: () => clearTimeout(timer) }
42. }
43. 
44. function wait(ms: number, signal?: AbortSignal) {
45.   return new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
46.     if (signal?.aborted) {
47.       reject(new DOMException("Aborted", "AbortError"))
48.       return
49.     }
50.     const timer = setTimeout(() => {
51.       signal?.removeEventListener("abort", onAbort)
52.       resolve()
53.     }, ms)
54.     const onAbort = () => {
55.       clearTimeout(timer)
56.       reject(new DOMException("Aborted", "AbortError"))
57.     }
58.     signal?.addEventListener("abort", onAbort, { once: true })
59.   })
60. }
61. 
62. function retryable(error: unknown, signal?: AbortSignal) {
63.   if (signal?.aborted) return false
64.   if (!(error instanceof Error)) return false
65.   if (error.name === "AbortError" || error.name === "TimeoutError") return false
66.   if (error instanceof TypeError) return true
67.   return /network|fetch|econnreset|econnrefused|enotfound|timedout/i.test(error.message)
68. }
69. 
70. export async function checkServerHealth(
71.   server: ServerConnection.HttpBase,
72.   fetch: typeof globalThis.fetch,
73.   opts?: CheckServerHealthOptions,
74. ): Promise<ServerHealth> {
75.   const timeout = opts?.signal ? undefined : timeoutSignal(opts?.timeoutMs ?? defaultTimeoutMs)
76.   const signal = opts?.signal ?? timeout?.signal
77.   const retryCount = opts?.retryCount ?? defaultRetryCount
78.   const retryDelayMs = opts?.retryDelayMs ?? defaultRetryDelayMs
79.   const next = (count: number, error: unknown) => {
80.     if (count >= retryCount || !retryable(error, signal)) return Promise.resolve({ healthy: false } as const)
81.     return wait(retryDelayMs * (count + 1), signal)
82.       .then(() => attempt(count + 1))
83.       .catch(() => ({ healthy: false }))
84.   }
85.   const attempt = (count: number): Promise<ServerHealth> =>
86.     createSdkForServer({
87.       server,
88.       fetch,
89.       signal,
90.     })
91.       .global.health()
92.       .then((x) => (x.error ? next(count, x.error) : { healthy: x.data?.healthy === true, version: x.data?.version }))
93.       .catch((error) => next(count, error))
94.   return attempt(0).finally(() => timeout?.clear?.())
95. }
96. 
97. const pollMs = 10_000
98. 
99. export function useCheckServerHealth() {
100.   const platform = usePlatform()
101.   const fetcher = platform.fetch ?? globalThis.fetch
102. 
103.   return (http: ServerConnection.HttpBase) => {
104.     const key = cacheKey(http)
105.     const hit = healthCache.get(key)
106.     const now = Date.now()
107.     if (hit && hit.fetch === fetcher && (!hit.done || now - hit.at < cacheMs)) return hit.promise
108.     const promise = checkServerHealth(http, fetcher).finally(() => {
109.       const next = healthCache.get(key)
110.       if (!next || next.promise !== promise) return
111.       next.done = true
112.       next.at = Date.now()
113.     })
114.     healthCache.set(key, { at: now, done: false, fetch: fetcher, promise })
115.     return promise
116.   }
117. }
118. 
119. export const useServerHealth = (servers: Accessor<ServerConnection.Any[]>, enabled: Accessor<boolean>) => {
120.   const checkServerHealth = useCheckServerHealth()
121.   const [status, setStatus] = createStore({} as Record<ServerConnection.Key, ServerHealth | undefined>)
122. 
123.   createEffect(() => {
124.     if (!enabled()) {
125.       setStatus(reconcile({}))
126.       return
127.     }
128.     const list = servers()
129.     let dead = false
130. 
131.     const refresh = async () => {
132.       const results: Record<string, ServerHealth> = {}
133.       await Promise.all(
134.         list.map(async (conn) => {
135.           const key = ServerConnection.key(conn)
136.           const result = await checkServerHealth(conn.http)
137.           results[key] = result
138.           if (!dead) setStatus(key, result)
139.         }),
140.       )
141.       if (dead) return
142.       setStatus(reconcile(results))
143.     }
144. 
145.     void refresh()
146.     const id = setInterval(() => void refresh(), pollMs)
147.     onCleanup(() => {
148.       dead = true
149.       clearInterval(id)
150.     })
151.   })
152. 
153.   return status
154. }
155.

### 💭 Reasoning

*I see the issue now. The user is using the web UI and the web UI has a server add/connection dialog that shows a password field. But I think the user might mean something different by "asks for a password" - maybe the browser pops up a native Basic Auth dialog.

Let me look at the authorization middleware more carefully to understand when/where the www-authenticate header is sent and whether it could happen when no password is configured.

Wait, actually re-reading the problem statement: "but it ask for password, even though I didn't set OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD". This is about the browser showing a Basic Auth dialog.

Let me trace through the code:


1. `opencode serve --hostname 0.0.0.0 --port 4096 --mdns` starts the server
2. User opens the web UI in browser
3. Browser shows a password prompt

For the browser to show a Basic Auth dialog, the server must be returning 401 with `www-authenticate: Basic realm="..."` header.

Looking at the auth middleware...

Examining the credential validation logic...

Both the validation function and router middleware check whether authentication is actually required. If no password is configured, the auth check gets skipped entirely.

But I'm realizing the real issue might be with how Effect's ConfigProvider works. There's a caching mechanism that snapshots environment variables on first read and holds onto that snapshot. The auth middleware layers are defined at the module level in server.ts, which means they're reading the config once when the module loads. If the password environment variable changes after that initial read, the cached config won't reflect the update, so the auth check still thinks a password is required even though it's no longer set. Actually, I think I'm overcomplicating this. The user said they're getting a password prompt even though they didn't set `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, but looking at the serve command, it should warn that the server is unsecured when that variable isn't set. So either the warning isn't showing up, or there's an existing password file being picked up somewhere that I need to investigate.

Looking at how the server initializes its config provider, it's setting up a fresh `ConfigProvider` that reads from the current environment variables for each listener, rather than caching them at module load time. This ensures that environment changes are reflected properly. But I'm wondering if the `opencode.json` config file might have a server password field that's being merged in and overriding the environment check. Each listener gets its own fresh memoMap via `makeMemoMapUnsafe()`, so that's not the caching culprit. I'm wondering if the issue is actually coming from somewhere else entirely—maybe the TUI itself is prompting for a password when it connects to the server, or perhaps it's a web UI dialog rather than browser Basic Auth. Let me trace through how the TUI establishes its connection and when that password dialog might appear. I think the real issue is that when accessing the server remotely via mDNS (like `http://opencode.local:4096`), the web UI displays a "connect to server" dialog that includes a password field, even though the server doesn't actually require authentication when `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't set.

Let me trace through how the mDNS discovery and UI authentication flow work to understand why this password prompt is appearing.*

1. import { Bonjour } from "bonjour-service"
2. 
3. let bonjour: Bonjour | undefined
4. let currentPort: number | undefined
5. 
6. export function publish(port: number, domain?: string) {
7.   if (currentPort === port) return
8.   if (bonjour) unpublish()
9. 
10.   try {
11.     const host = domain ?? "opencode.local"
12.     const name = `opencode-${port}`
13.     bonjour = new Bonjour()
14.     const service = bonjour.publish({
15.       name,
16.       type: "http",
17.       host,
18.       port,
19.       txt: { path: "/" },
20.     })
21. 
22.     service.on("error", () => {})
23. 
24.     currentPort = port
25.   } catch {
26.     if (bonjour) {
27.       try {
28.         bonjour.destroy()
29.       } catch {}
30.     }
31.     bonjour = undefined
32.     currentPort = undefined
33.   }
34. }
35. 
36. export function unpublish() {
37.   if (bonjour) {
38.     try {
39.       bonjour.unpublishAll()
40.       bonjour.destroy()
41.     } catch {}
42.     bonjour = undefined
43.     currentPort = undefined
44.   }
45. }
46. 
47. export * as MDNS from "./mdns"
48.

### 💭 Reasoning

*OK, so the auth middleware correctly checks `ServerAuth.required(config)` before enforcing auth. If no password is set, it returns pass-through. 

Now let me look at the actual request flow: when a browser accesses the server, what happens when the server returns 401? Does the UI show a login page?

Actually, wait. Let me check if the web UI has some kind of client-side authentication check. When the browser connects to the server and gets a 401 for some reason, the browser might show a Basic Auth dialog. But since `ServerAuth.required()` returns false when no password is set, 401 should NOT be returned.
 
I'm wondering if the password might be persisting from a previous configuration—either through an environment variable, a `.env` file, or stored in the `opencode.json` config file itself. Let me check how the Config system loads the password and whether `server.password` is actually a supported field.

Looking at the server startup flow, when `opencode serve` runs, it goes through `Server.listen()` which sets up the HTTP routes and auth layers. The auth layer uses `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` to read `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` via Effect's `Config.option`. I need to understand how `Config.option` actually behaves when the environment variable is set.

If `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD=""` (empty string), then `Config.string` succeeds with an empty string, `Config.option` wraps it as `Some("")`, and `ServerAuth.required()` checks if the password is non-empty—so it correctly doesn't require auth. Even with a space like `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD=" "`, the auth would kick in since a space isn't an empty string. But maybe the real issue is something else entirely about how the config is being resolved.

Looking at the server setup, the ConfigProvider only reads from `process.env`—it doesn't load from `opencode.json`. So if `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't in the environment, it would be `None`. I should check whether there's any `.env` file loading happening that might be providing this value.*

<sub>⏱️ 7m 8s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*I'm not finding a specific "requires password" UI flow in the app. Let me think about this differently.

The user says they run `opencode serve --hostname 0.0.0.0 --port 4096 --mdns` and the server asks for a password even though `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is not set.

Let me re-read the code path very carefully:

1. `opencode serve` runs `ServeCommand` in `packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/serve.ts`
2. The serve command first checks `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and warns if not set


3. Then it calls `Server.listen(opts)` which chains through several layers: `listenEffect` → `startWithPortFallback` → `startListener` with the specified port
4. `startListener` builds the listener layer by composing the HTTP router with the API routes and providing the environment configuration
5. `HttpApiApp.createRoutes(opts)` applies module-level auth layers like `authOnlyRouterLayer` and `httpApiAuthLayer` that use `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`
6. These auth layers check for `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` via configuration, and if it's not set, the password is `None` and `required()` returns `false`, meaning no auth is enforced
7. But wait—I'm looking at those module-level constants in `server.ts` and realizing something might be off with how the auth layers are being composed and provided
8. The key issue is that Effect's default `ConfigProvider` snapshots `process.env` at module load time and caches it, so if these module-level constants get evaluated before a fresh `ConfigProvider` is supplied, the cached environment might not reflect the current state

. But `Layer.provide` is lazy—it doesn't evaluate until the layer is built. When `listenerLayer` is constructed with the fresh `ConfigProvider`, that provider should propagate through the entire layer tree, including to `authOnlyRouterLayer` and its dependencies.

The key is that `startListener` creates a fresh memoization map for each build, so module-level auth layers aren't cached across listener instances. This means each time a listener starts, it should get a fresh evaluation of the auth configuration based on the current environment variables.

So in theory, when `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't set, the password auth should be disabled. Let me reconsider this from another angle.

Wait, maybe the issue isn't with the HTTP auth at all. The user might be running the desktop app or CLI, not the web UI. When they run `opencode serve` with those flags and then try to connect, the CLI or TUI might be prompting for a password instead of the server itself.

Let me check how the TUI command connects to the server to understand what's actually asking for the password.*

<sub>⏱️ 7m 44s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see! In `worker.ts`:

```ts
async fetch(input: { url: string; method: string; headers: Record\<string, string\>; body?: string }) {
  const headers = { ...input.headers }
  const auth = ServerAuth.header()
  if (auth && !headers["authorization"] && !headers["Authorization"]) {
    headers["Authorization"] = auth
  }
```

This `ServerAuth.header()` is called WITHOUT passing credentials, so it falls back to `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`:

```ts
export function header(credentials?: Credentials) {
  const password = credentials?.password ?? Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD


```

If the environment variable isn't set, `auth` returns undefined and no authorization header gets added—which is fine for the TUI. Looking at the `server` RPC call, when the TUI requests it, the server starts up and returns its URL. The server then uses `createRoutes` to set up its endpoints.

Let me step back and think about this differently. Maybe the user is hitting the web UI through a browser and seeing a login prompt because the server is returning a 401 response. I should check what the `/global/health` endpoint returns when auth isn't configured—if it's returning 401, the browser would automatically show a Basic Auth dialog. I'm also noticing `fenceLayer` in the server configuration, so let me investigate what that does.*

```
import { Flag } from "@opencode-ai/core/flag/flag"
import { Database } from "@opencode-ai/core/database/database"
import { Effect } from "effect"
import { HttpRouter, HttpServerRequest, HttpServerResponse } from "effect/unstable/http"
import * as Fence from "@/server/shared/fence"

const ignoredMethods = new Set(["GET", "HEAD", "OPTIONS"])

export const fenceLayer = HttpRouter.middleware<{ requires: Database.Service; handles: unknown }>()(
  Effect.gen(function* () {
    const { db } = yield* Database.Service
    return (effect) =>
      Effect.gen(function* () {
        const request = yield* HttpServerRequest.HttpServerRequest
        if (!Flag.OPENCODE_WORKSPACE_ID || ignoredMethods.has(request.method)) return yield* effect

        const previous = yield* Fence.load(db)
        const response = yield* effect
        const current = Fence.diff(previous, yield* Fence.load(db))
        if (Object.keys(current).length === 0) return response

        return HttpServerResponse.setHeader(response, Fence.HEADER, JSON.stringify(current))
      })
  }),
).layer
<shellId: 19 completed with exit code 0>
```

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I have a good understanding of the codebase. Let me analyze the problem:

The user runs `opencode serve --hostname 0.0.0.0 --port 4096 --mdns` but the server asks for a password even though `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is not set.

Looking at the code in `packages/opencode/src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/server.ts`:

```ts
const authOnlyRouterLayer = authorizationRouterMiddleware.layer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
const httpApiAuthLayer = authorizationLayer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))


```

These module-level constants are setting up authorization layers that use `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`, which reads the `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` from Effect's ConfigProvider. The issue is that this configuration is being set up at module load time, but there's a comment in the listening server code about installing a fresh `ConfigProvider` per listener so that `Config.string(...)` reads reflect the current `process.env` state.

The problem is that Effect's default ConfigProvider snapshots `process.env` on first read and caches it, so without providing a fresh `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` per listener, every subsequent `Server.listen()` call would keep observing that initial snapshot. Since `Layer` is lazy in Effect, the module-level auth layers shouldn't actually be evaluated until `startListener` builds them with `Layer.buildWithMemoMap`, at which point they should use the fresh ConfigProvider.

But I'm realizing the bug might be more subtle than that. Let me trace through this more carefully.
```

ts
const authOnlyRouterLayer = authorizationRouterMiddleware.layer.pipe(Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer))
```

This layer has `ConfigProvider` as a dependency. When `listenerLayer` provides `ConfigProvider.layer(ConfigProvider.fromEnv())`, that fresh provider should flow into `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`. The issue is that if Effect's module-level ConfigProvider singleton has already been initialized by some other code reading a config value early, it would have cached that initial `process.env` snapshot.

Actually, wait — the per-listener `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` is fresh and reads from the current `process.env`, so it should see the right values. Let me reconsider this from a different angle. What if the problem is with how `EffectConfig.option` works? Looking at the password config, it uses a function that fails with `ConfigError.MissingData` when the env var isn't set, then pipes through `EffectConfig.option` to transform that error into `Option.none()`. That should be the correct behavior — if the password isn't set, the config should be `None`. But maybe `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` doesn't properly propagate `MissingData` errors to `Config.option`, or there's something specific about how it handles undefined values from `process.env`. If `process.env.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is undefined, the string config should error, which `option` converts to `None`. Theoretically that's all working as expected, so the server should...

Actually, wait — the user might have `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` set somewhere they're not aware of, like in their shell configuration files or environment setup. Or maybe there's a different code path I'm missing. Let me trace through what happens when they run the serve command with those flags. The CLI command logs a warning if the password isn't set, then calls into the server startup logic.

If the browser is showing a Basic Auth dialog, that means the server is returning a 401 response with the `www-authenticate` header. For that to happen, the auth layer must think a password is required — which should only occur if `config.password` is actually set to something. So either the `ConfigProvider` is pulling the password from somewhere unexpected, or there's a bug in how the config option is being handled. Let me look more closely at how the environment configuration is being read. `HttpApiApp.context` is just an empty context map, so the `ConfigProvider` has to come from the layer stack itself. When I provide a `ConfigProvider` layer, it makes that service available to all the sub-layers that depend on it. I'm realizing I need to trace through how `Layer.buildWithMemoMap` actually resolves these dependencies with the fresh memoMap and scope.

Let me step back and look at the actual test to see if there's already a case that verifies no authentication is required when the environment variable isn't set. The test is setting up a layer with no password configured and checking that requests succeed with a 200 status.

But this test bypasses the full server initialization path—it's directly providing the auth config layer rather than going through the `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` flow. So there's no test covering the scenario where the environment variable is absent and the default config provider gets used.

I'm noticing that the module-level setup uses `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` to provide the auth configuration...

Actually, I need to think through how `Layer.provide` works here. When `authOnlyRouterLayer` wraps `authorizationRouterMiddleware.layer` with `Layer.provide(ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer)`, the resulting layer should no longer require `ConfigProvider` as a dependency since it's being satisfied by the provided layer. But I need to verify whether `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` actually declares `ConfigProvider` as a requirement in the first place.

Let me just run the server locally and see what actually happens instead of going in circles on this.

Actually, I need to trace through when `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer` gets evaluated. The layer is built with `Layer.buildWithMemoMap` using an empty context, which means it creates its own `ConfigProvider` instance at build time. The HTTP server and its request handlers are baked in with whatever auth configuration exists at that moment. So if the environment variable isn't set when the layer builds, the auth will be disabled regardless of what happens later.

But the user is seeing a password prompt, which suggests something else is providing that configuration. Maybe their `opencode.json` file has a password field, or the `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` environment variable is actually set in their environment and they're not aware of it. So the server's returning 401, which means `ServerAuth.required(config)` is evaluating to true — that only happens if `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is actually set to something non-empty. Either the user has it configured somewhere they're not aware of, or there's a bug in how the config is being loaded per-listener that's causing it to fall back to a cached or default state. Let me check the mDNS test file to see if there's something specific about how the password handling works in that flow.*

140. function waitForMessage(ws: WebSocket, predicate: (message: string) => boolean) {
141.   const decoder = new TextDecoder()
142.   let onMessage: ((event: MessageEvent) => void) | undefined
143.   return withTimeout(

450. function isPortFree(port: number) {
451.   return new Promise<boolean>((resolve) => {
452.     const probe = net.createServer()
453.     probe.once("error", () => resolve(false))
454.     probe.once("listening", () => probe.close(() => resolve(true)))
455.     probe.listen(port, "127.0.0.1")
456.   })
457. }
458. 
459. function occupyPort(port: number) {
460.   return new Promise<net.Server | undefined>((resolve) => {
461.     const server = net.createServer()
462.     server.once("error", () => resolve(undefined))
463.     server.listen(port, "127.0.0.1", () => resolve(server))
464.   })
465. }

<sub>⏱️ 9m 35s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see the issue! In `httpapi-listen.test.ts`, the `startNoAuthListener` function:

```ts
async function startNoAuthListener() {
  Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD = undefined
  Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME = auth.username
  delete process.env.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD
  process.env.OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME = auth.username
  return Server.listen({ hostname: "127.0.0.1", port: 0 })}```

It sets BOTH `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD = undefined` AND `delete process.env.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`. 

So there are two separate sources that need clearing: the `Flag` object itself and the actual environment variables. The `Flag.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` gets populated from `process.env` at module load time, which is why both need to be cleared to properly disable authentication in the test. The tests confirm the server should work without auth when those variables aren't set, so the user's experience of being prompted for a password is strange. Let me check if the desktop app might be handling the password differently or enforcing auth through a different mechanism.*

---

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I have a very clear picture. Looking at the desktop `server.ts`, line 55-57:
