But the user is running `opencode serve` directly, not through the desktop app. The `opencode serve` command should respect `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` - if not set, no auth required.

Looking at `packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/serve.ts`:```ts


Checking for the password flag...

The code logs a warning when `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` isn't set, which means the server runs without authentication. If the user isn't seeing this warning, the environment variable must already be set somewhere. They might be running the command from within the desktop app's environment, or it could be inherited from their shell configuration.

Looking at how the desktop app handles this, it sets `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` specifically in the sidecar process environment through a preparation function, not in the main process. But if the user is running the command from a terminal spawned by the desktop app itself, that terminal would inherit the password variable. Let me reconsider this. The real issue is that `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is set somewhere in their environment, and both the serve and web commands check for it and warn if it's missing. If it's present, the server requires authentication. The most straightforward explanation is that this environment variable is being set somewhere in their system, likely inherited from the sidecar process or persisting from a previous session. Let me reconsider how `Config.option` actually handles missing environment variables in Effect. If `Config.string` returns `MissingData` for an unset env var, then wrapping it with `Config.option` should convert that to `None`. But I'm wondering if there's a different error type being thrown that wouldn't get caught by `Config.option`, or if I'm misunderstanding the behavior entirely. Maybe I should stop second-guessing the implementation and focus on what the user is actually experiencing.

Re-reading the issue, the user says they're running the server without setting `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, yet they're being prompted for one. This might not be about the HTTP server auth at all—it could be that when accessing the web UI remotely via mDNS, the client-side UI is showing a password field as part of a server connection dialog, which the user interprets as the server asking for authentication.

Let me trace through what actually happens when the server starts. The `serve` command checks if the password environment variable is set and logs a warning if it's not, then it initializes the server with the network options and starts listening. I need to look at what `Server.listen` does and whether there's any authentication middleware being applied...

The routes are created through `createRoutes` from the HTTP API app, which uses authentication layers that read from `ServerAuth.Config.defaultLayer`. This layer gets its configuration from a fresh `ConfigProvider` that reads directly from `process.env` each time the listener is called. So the real question is whether `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is actually present in the environment when the server starts up. Looking at the desktop sidecar...

Setting environment variables...

So when a user opens an embedded terminal in the desktop app and runs `opencode serve`, they inherit the sidecar's environment variables, including `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`. That's the bug—the password gets passed down to the child process.

I could fix this by clearing that variable from PTY child processes, documenting the behavior, or adding a flag to disable auth. But wait, maybe the user is just running this from their regular terminal where the password was set in a previous session. Let me look at the serve command logic to understand what's actually happening.

The code checks if `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is set at module load time, and if it is, the server requires authentication. If the user is running `opencode serve` from within the embedded terminal, they'd inherit this environment variable automatically from the desktop app. So when they say they didn't set the password themselves, they're right—but the app is setting it for them behind the scenes. This is definitely a bug because users don't realize their server is requiring auth, and the warning about an unsecured server never appears since the variable is already set.

Actually, I'm second-guessing myself on whether this is the exact scenario. Let me reconsider what's actually happening here.

The user is running the correct command with `--hostname 0.0.0.0 --mdns`, which means the server is accessible from other devices on the network. When they say the server "asks for password," they might be accessing it from the opencode app on another device, and the app is prompting for authentication. Or they might not realize how the password mechanism works in the URL itself.

Looking more carefully at the actual problem: the user didn't set `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` but the server is still asking for one. The warning message that appears when the password isn't set is the key clue here. If the password environment variable was inherited from the desktop app, the user wouldn't see that warning—they'd just see the server running but requiring auth when accessed. This could be a legitimate bug where the desktop app starts a sidecar with a random UUID password, and when the user opens an embedded terminal, it inherits that `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` environment variable.

So the scenario is: user runs `opencode serve` in the embedded terminal, the new server inherits the password from the parent process, and now it requires authentication with a password the user never set and doesn't know. There are a few ways to fix this—either the `serve` command should explicitly unset the password unless provided via a flag, or the embedded terminal should clear it before spawning, or the sidecar should use a different authentication mechanism entirely rather than relying on environment variables.

<sub>⏱️ 11m 4s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I see the full picture. The `packages/desktop/src/main/sidecar.ts` sets `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` in the environment:

```tsfunction prepareSidecarEnv(password: string, userDataPath: string) {
  Object.assign(process.env, {    ...
    OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD: password,
    ...
  })}```

This sets `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` in the SIDECAR process's environment (the server process spawned by the desktop app). The PTY (embedded terminal) is spawned within this server process, so any PTY sessions inherit `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`.When the user runs `opencode serve` from within the embedded terminal, `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is already in the environment (inherited from the sidecar), and the new server they're starting requires auth.


So the root cause is that these credentials leak from the sidecar into child processes through the PTY. I need to strip `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and `OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` from the environment when spawning the PTY so embedded terminals don't inherit the server's auth credentials. Let me find where the PTY process is being spawned to apply this fix.*

---

115.   readonly remove: (id: PtyID) => Effect.Effect<void, NotFoundError>
116.   readonly write: (id: PtyID, data: string) => Effect.Effect<void, NotFoundError>
117.   readonly attach: (id: PtyID, input: AttachInput) => Effect.Effect<Attachment, NotFoundError | ExitedError>
118. }
119. 
120. export class Service extends Context.Service<Service, Interface>()("@opencode/v2/Pty") {}
121. 
122. export const layer = Layer.effect(
123.   Service,
124.   Effect.gen(function* () {
125.     const events = yield* EventV2.Service
126.     const location = yield* Location.Service
127.     const config = yield* Config.Service
128.     const context = yield* Effect.context()
129.     const runFork = Effect.runForkWith(context)
130.     const sessions = new Map<PtyID, Active>()
131.     const exitOrder: PtyID[] = []
132. 
133.     function notifyEnd(session: Active, event: { exitCode?: number }) {
134.       for (const subscriber of session.subscribers.values()) {
135.         if (!subscriber.active) {
136.           subscriber.end = event
137.           continue
138.         }
139.         try {
140.           subscriber.onEnd(event)
141.         } catch {}
142.       }
143.       session.subscribers.clear()
144.     }
145. 
146.     function teardown(session: Active) {
147.       for (const listener of session.listeners) listener.dispose()
148.       session.listeners.length = 0
149.       if (session.info.status === "running") {
150.         try {
151.           session.process.kill()
152.         } catch {}
153.       }
154.       notifyEnd(session, {})
155.     }
156. 
157.     yield* Effect.addFinalizer(() =>
158.       Effect.sync(() => {
159.         for (const session of sessions.values()) teardown(session)
160.         sessions.clear()
161.         exitOrder.length = 0
162.       }),
163.     )
164. 
165.     const requireSession = Effect.fn("Pty.requireSession")(function* (id: PtyID) {
166.       const session = sessions.get(id)
167.       if (!session) return yield* new NotFoundError({ ptyID: id })
168.       return session
169.     })
170. 
171.     const removeSession = Effect.fnUntraced(function* (id: PtyID) {
172.       const session = sessions.get(id)
173.       if (!session) return
174.       sessions.delete(id)
175.       const index = exitOrder.indexOf(id)
176.       if (index !== -1) exitOrder.splice(index, 1)
177.       yield* Effect.logInfo("removing session", { id })
178.       teardown(session)
179.       yield* events.publish(Event.Deleted, { id: session.info.id })
180.     })
181. 
182.     const remove = Effect.fn("Pty.remove")(function* (id: PtyID) {
183.       yield* requireSession(id)
184.       yield* removeSession(id)
185.     })
186. 
187.     const list = Effect.fn("Pty.list")(function* () {
188.       return Array.from(sessions.values()).map((session) => session.info)
189.     })
190. 
191.     const get = Effect.fn("Pty.get")(function* (id: PtyID) {
192.       return (yield* requireSession(id)).info
193.     })
194. 
195.     const create = Effect.fn("Pty.create")(function* (input: CreateInput) {
196.       const id = PtyID.ascending()
197.       const command = input.command || Shell.preferred(Config.latest(yield* config.entries(), "shell"))
198.       const args = Shell.login(command) ? [...(input.args ?? []), "-l"] : [...(input.args ?? [])]
199.       const cwd = input.cwd || location.directory
200.       const env = {
201.         ...process.env,
202.         ...input.env,
203.         TERM: "xterm-256color",
204.         OPENCODE_TERMINAL: "1",
205.       } as Record<string, string>
206.       if (process.platform === "win32") {
207.         env.LC_ALL = "C.UTF-8"
208.         env.LC_CTYPE = "C.UTF-8"
209.         env.LANG = "C.UTF-8"
210.       }
211.       yield* Effect.logInfo("creating session", { id, cmd: command, args, cwd })
212.       const { spawn } = yield* Effect.promise(() => pty())
213.       const proc = yield* Effect.sync(() => spawn(command, args, { name: "xterm-256color", cwd, env }))
214.       const info: Info = {
215.         id,
216.         title: input.title || `Terminal ${id.slice(-4)}`,
217.         command,
218.         args,
219.         cwd,
220.         status: "running",
221.         pid: proc.pid,
222.       }
223.       const session: Active = {
224.         info,
225.         process: proc,
226.         buffer: "",
227.         bufferCursor: 0,
228.         cursor: 0,
229.         subscribers: new Map(),
230.         listeners: [],
231.       }
232.       sessions.set(id, session)
233.       session.listeners.push(
234.         proc.onData((chunk) => {
235.           session.cursor += chunk.length
236.           for (const [token, subscriber] of session.subscribers.entries()) {
237.             if (!subscriber.active) {
238.               subscriber.pending.push(chunk)
239.               continue
240.             }
241.             try {
242.               subscriber.onData(chunk)
243.             } catch {
244.               session.subscribers.delete(token)
245.             }
246.           }
247.           session.buffer += chunk
248.           if (session.buffer.length <= BUFFER_LIMIT) return
249.           const excess = session.buffer.length - BUFFER_LIMIT
250.           session.buffer = session.buffer.slice(excess)
251.           session.bufferCursor += excess
252.         }),
253.         proc.onExit(({ exitCode }) => {
254.           if (session.info.status === "exited") return
255.           session.info.status = "exited"
256.           session.info.exitCode = exitCode
257.           notifyEnd(session, { exitCode })
258.           exitOrder.push(id)
259.           runFork(
260.             Effect.gen(function* () {
261.               yield* Effect.logInfo("session exited", { id, exitCode })
262.               yield* events.publish(Event.Exited, { id, exitCode })
263.               while (exitOrder.length > EXITED_LIMIT) {
264.                 const oldest = exitOrder[0]
265.                 if (!oldest) break
266.                 yield* removeSession(oldest)
267.               }
268.             }),
269.           )
270.         }),
271.       )
272.       yield* events.publish(Event.Created, { info })
273.       return info
274.     })
275. 
276.     const update = Effect.fn("Pty.update")(function* (id: PtyID, input: UpdateInput) {
277.       const session = yield* requireSession(id)
278.       if (input.title) session.info.title = input.title
279.       if (input.size && session.info.status === "running") session.process.resize(input.size.cols, input.size.rows)
280.       yield* events.publish(Event.Updated, { info: session.info })
281.       return session.info
282.     })
283. 
284.     const write = Effect.fn("Pty.write")(function* (id: PtyID, data: string) {
285.       const session = yield* requireSession(id)
286.       if (session.info.status === "running") session.process.write(data)
287.     })
288. 
289.     const attach = Effect.fn("Pty.attach")(function* (id: PtyID, input: AttachInput) {
290.       const session = yield* requireSession(id)
291.       if (session.info.status !== "running") return yield* new ExitedError({ ptyID: id })
292.       yield* Effect.logInfo("client attached to session", { id, directory: location.directory })
293.       const token = {}
294.       const subscriber: Subscriber = {
295.         onData: input.onData,
296.         onEnd: input.onEnd,
297.         active: false,
298.         detached: false,
299.         pending: [],
300.       }
301.       session.subscribers.set(token, subscriber)
302.       const start = session.bufferCursor
303.       const end = session.cursor
304.       const from =
305.         input.cursor === -1
306.           ? end
307.           : typeof input.cursor === "number" && Number.isSafeInteger(input.cursor)
308.             ? Math.max(0, input.cursor)
309.             : 0
310.       const replay = (() => {
311.         if (!session.buffer || from >= end) return ""
312.         const offset = Math.max(0, from - start)
313.         if (offset >= session.buffer.length) return ""
314.         return session.buffer.slice(offset)
315.       })()
316.       return {
317.         replay,
318.         cursor: end,
319.         write: (data: string) => {
320.           if (session.info.status === "running") session.process.write(data)
321.         },
322.         activate: () => {
323.           if (subscriber.active || subscriber.detached) return
324.           subscriber.active = true
325.           try {
326.             for (const chunk of subscriber.pending) subscriber.onData(chunk)
327.             subscriber.pending.length = 0
328.             if (subscriber.end) subscriber.onEnd(subscriber.end)
329.           } catch {
330.             session.subscribers.delete(token)
331.           }
332.         },
333.         detach: () => {
334.           subscriber.detached = true
335.           subscriber.pending.length = 0
336.           subscriber.end = undefined
337.           session.subscribers.delete(token)
338.         },
339.       }
340.     })
341. 
342.     return Service.of({ list, get, create, update, remove, write, attach })
343.   }),
344. )
345. 
346. export const locationLayer = layer.pipe(Layer.provide(Config.locationLayer))
347.
```

</details>

<sub>⏱️ 11m 39s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Found it! In `packages/core/src/pty.ts`, the `create` function at line 200:

```ts
const env = {
  ...process.env,
  ...input.env,
  TERM: "xterm-256color",
  OPENCODE_TERMINAL: "1",
} as Record\<string, string\>
```

This spreads `process.env` into the PTY environment, which includes `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` if it's set in the sidecar's environment.

The fix is to strip `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and `OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` from the PTY environment so that embedded terminal sessions don't inherit the server's credentials.


So the root cause is that when the desktop sidecar runs, it sets these credentials in its environment, and then any terminal spawned from the PTY inherits them. When users run `opencode serve` inside that terminal, the new server instance picks up those inherited credentials and requires authentication. I need to filter out these sensitive variables before constructing the PTY environment.

Modifying the env assembly in the `create` function... I'm reconsidering whether deleting those environment variables is the right call. If someone explicitly passes `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` through `input.env`, we'd still remove it, which might be the intended behavior to keep credentials out of PTY processes—but I should think through whether that's actually what we want. I need to verify whether `OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` is actually being set by the sidecar. Looking at the code, I see the password is set in `prepareSidecarEnv`, but I'm not finding where the username gets assigned. Let me check the test files to understand how both credentials are being passed around.

Since both password and username could potentially be exposed through the PTY environment, I should strip both from the environment variables to be safe. Now let me implement the fix and also verify the WSL sidecar to confirm it has the same vulnerability.

Looking at the WSL sidecar code, I can see it's exporting the password directly into the shell environment, which would leak to any PTY processes spawned from there. So the solution is clear: remove both `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and `OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` from PTY process environments.

Beyond the PTY terminals, I need to check if there are other places where child processes might inherit these credentials. The general process spawning utility also merges environment variables, so I should examine whether that needs protection too. The PTY creation function spreads all environment variables including `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, which means it leaks to the terminal session. I should strip that specific variable when constructing the environment for the PTY, similar to how we'd handle it for other child processes. Let me implement that fix now. they might want them, but that's unlikely and risky. I'll go with always stripping them. My fix is to delete `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and `OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` from the environment object after merging in `pty.ts`, which prevents the server's credentials from leaking into user terminals.

I'm also checking what other sensitive variables the sidecar sets—`OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is the main culprit, while `OPENCODE_CLIENT` and `XDG_STATE_HOME` aren't sensitive. The WSL case is trickier since the shell environment would have the password set, but the `pty.ts` fix handles the non-WSL case for now.

I'm going to focus on the PTY terminals for this fix rather than touching `process.ts`, which affects formatters and LSP, then implement the change and run tests.*

The desktop sidecar sets `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` in its own `process.env`, which leaks into every embedded terminal. Users running `opencode serve` from that terminal inherit the password and get locked out.

### ✅ `bash`

**Commit the fix**

$ cd /Users/engineer/workspace/opencode && git add packages/core/src/pty.ts && git commit -m "fix(core): strip server credentials from PTY child environments

The desktop sidecar sets OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD (and optionally
OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME) directly in process.env so the embedded
opencode server requires authentication. Because PTY terminals are
spawned by spreading process.env, every shell session inherits those
variables. Any 'opencode serve' the user runs from the embedded terminal
silently picks up the sidecar password and requires authentication,
even though the user never configured one themselves.

Fix: delete OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD and OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME from
the assembled PTY environment after the spread so the credentials stay
internal to the server process and never reach user-facing terminals.

Co-authored-by: Copilot <223556219+Copilot@users.noreply.github.com>"

[fix-post-rebase-build-errors 04bf68101] fix(core): strip server credentials from PTY child environments
 1 file changed, 6 insertions(+)

<sub>⏱️ 14m 6s</sub>

### 💬 Copilot

**Root cause:** The desktop sidecar injects `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` into its own `process.env` so the embedded server requires auth. PTY terminals were spawned with `...process.env`, so the embedded shell inherited that variable. Running `opencode serve` from that terminal picked up the sidecar's auto-generated password — requiring auth you never set.

**Fix:** `packages/core/src/pty.ts` — after assembling the PTY env, `delete env.OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and `delete env.OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME`. All 11 listen tests pass.

---

### ✅ `bash`

**Create upstream PR**

$ cd /Users/engineer/workspace/opencode && gh pr create \
  --repo anomalyco/opencode \
  --head dzianisv:pty-strip-server-creds \
  --base dev \
  --title "fix(core): strip server credentials from PTY child environments" \
  --body "## Problem

The desktop sidecar sets \`OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD\` directly in \`process.env\` so the embedded server requires authentication. PTY terminals are spawned with \`...process.env\`, so every embedded shell session inherits that variable.

When a user runs \`opencode serve\` (or \`opencode web\`) from the embedded terminal, the new server silently picks up the sidecar's auto-generated password and requires authentication — even though the user never configured one themselves. The \`OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD is not set; server is unsecured\` warning is also suppressed because the variable *is* set (via inheritance).

## Fix

Delete \`OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD\` and \`OPENCODE_SERVER_USERNAME\` from the assembled PTY environment after the spread so the sidecar's credentials stay internal to the server process and never reach user-facing terminals.

## Testing

Existing test \`keeps PTY websocket tickets optional when server auth is disabled\` (httpapi-listen.test.ts) still passes. All 11 listen tests pass." 2>&1

<details>
<summary>120 lines</summary>

````
# agents-supervisor

A dataset-backed **supervisor** for coding agents. It watches for the agent
stopping **prematurely** — asking permission for work it could just do, listing
"next steps" then halting, or stalling mid-task — and re-prompts it to finish.
Always-on, low-risk (it approves legitimate stops and waits), and it learns from
your own sessions.

> Successor to the (now archived) [dzianisv/opencode-plugins](https://github.com/dzianisv/opencode-plugins) reflection plugin.

Works on two runtimes from one shared core:

| Runtime | Mechanism | Surface |
|---|---|---|
| **Claude Code** | `Stop` hook (`bin/on-stop.mjs`) | plugin + `/supervisor:train`, `/supervisor:status`, `/supervisor:goal`, `/supervisor:retry` |
| **OpenCode** | `session.idle` event (`opencode/supervisor.ts`) | tools `supervisor`, `set_supervisor`, `supervisor_train`, `supervisor_goal`, `supervisor_retry` + `/supervisor`, `/supervisor:train`, `/supervisor:goal`, `/supervisor:retry` |

The classification taxonomy (6 categories + mined anti-patterns) and its feedback
templates live in a single source of truth, [`core/patterns.json`](core/patterns.json),
so both runtimes — and the trainer — share one brain.

## Quickstart (1 minute)

### Claude Code

Run in your terminal:
```sh
claude plugin marketplace add dzianisv/agents-supervisor
claude plugin install supervisor@agents-supervisor
```
The `Stop` hook is active in the next session. Verify:
```sh
claude plugin list   # should show: supervisor@agents-supervisor  ✔ enabled
```

Then, inside any Claude Code session:
- `/supervisor:status` — effective patterns + recent verdicts
- `/supervisor:train` — learn from your sessions (updates your local patterns)

> **Slash commands vs CLI:** `claude plugin ...` runs from your terminal; `/plugin ...` is the equivalent inside an active Claude Code session. Both work — use whichever fits your context.

**Local dev install** (clone first, then):
```sh
claude plugin marketplace add /path/to/agents-supervisor
claude plugin install supervisor@agents-supervisor
```

**Switch off / on:**
```sh
# whole plugin (all sessions) — run in terminal
claude plugin disable supervisor@agents-supervisor
claude plugin enable  supervisor@agents-supervisor
```

Inside an active Claude Code session:
- `/supervisor:off` — disable for this session
- `/supervisor:on` — re-enable for this session

### OpenCode
Add to `opencode.json`:
```json
{ "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json", "plugin": ["opencode-supervisor"] }
```
or drop `opencode/supervisor.ts` into `~/.config/opencode/plugin/`. Then:
- `/supervisor` — status · `/supervisor:train` — learn (web app: call the `supervisor_train` tool; file commands don't expand in the web UI)

**Switch off / on:**
```
/supervisor off     # disable for this session
/supervisor on      # re-enable
```
Whole plugin off: remove `"opencode-supervisor"` from `opencode.json`, or launch with `opencode --pure`.

## Supervisor mode — goal loop (both runtimes)

Beyond catching premature stops, you can point the supervisor at a **goal** it must
demonstrably meet before it lets the agent stop — it keeps re-prompting (up to a
retry budget) until the goal's met or the budget runs out.

```
/supervisor:goal all tests in test/auth pass and the PR is open with green CI
/supervisor:goal              # check status (condition, attempts, last reason)
/supervisor:goal clear        # clear (aliases: stop, off, reset, none, cancel)
/supervisor:retry 24          # set this session's retry budget (1–100, default 16)
```

The goal is injected into the judge as a **mandatory completion requirement** — the
agent is not allowed to stop until the goal is met (or the budget exhausts). While a
goal is active the retry budget rises to 16 (vs 3 normally). State is per-session at
`.supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json` (mode 0600); budget is spent only when a
continuation actually fires.

- **Claude Code:** `/supervisor:goal` / `/supervisor:retry` skills (the `Stop` hook enforces it).
- **OpenCode:** same commands in the TUI; in the web app call the `supervisor_goal` / `supervisor_retry` tools.

**Configurable rubric (OpenCode):** drop a `.supervisor/rubric.md` (or
`~/.config/opencode/supervisor/rubric.md`) with `## Patterns` / `## Antipatterns`
sections to override the judge's rubric; otherwise the shipped default is used.
(Claude Code reads its rubric from `core/patterns.json` ⊕ your user-local patterns.)

## How it decides

A judge LLM classifies each stop into one of:
`complete`, `waiting_for_user_legitimate`, `tool_available_punt`,
`summary_drift_stop`, `genuinely_stuck`, `working`. Only the middle three inject a
continuation nudge (escalating over up to 3 attempts); the rest are left alone.
The anti-pattern rules that sharpen these (permission-seeking, stopped-with-todos,
false-complete, legitimate-stop) were mined from real agent stops where the user
had to reply.

## `/supervisor:train` — learn from your sessions

```
/supervisor:train                 # mine last 14d, update your local patterns
/supervisor:train --since=30d
/supervisor:train --dry-run       # preview the pattern diff, write nothing
/supervisor:train --push-hf       # also archive the private dataset to HuggingFace
```

It mines `agent stopped → user followed up` pairs from your OpenCode DBs and Claude
transcripts, derives refreshed anti-pattern weights + provenance, and writes them to
your **user-local** patterns file:

```
~/.config/agents-supervisor/patterns.json   # learned overrides (deep-merged over shipped)
```

Guarantees:
- **Never commits** to this repo / upstream — learning is user-side only.
- **Dataset stays private** — mined data lands in `.dataset/` (git-ignored) and,
  with `--push-hf`, a **private** HuggingFace dataset repo
  (`$SUPERVISOR_HF_DATASET`, default `dzianisv/agent-supervisor-stops`). Never in git.
- A `.bak` of the prior patterns is kept; revert with the printed command.

First `--push-hf` run needs `hf auth login` and `pip install -U huggingface_hub`.

## Pattern resolution

`core/patterns.mjs` deep-merges, later overriding earlier:
1. shipped `core/patterns.json` (read-only defaults)
2. `~/.config/agents-supervisor/patterns.json` (user, written by train)
3. `<project>/.supervisor/patterns.json` (project, `--scope=project`)

## Develop / test

```
npm test            # unit tests (core + hook + train derivation), node:test
npm run test:cc     # Claude Code end-to-end (real claude -p, no mocks)
npm run eval        # OpenCode judge eval (promptfoo)
```

// Lazy Sentry helper — reports errors without crashing if @sentry/node is unavailable
async function reportError(err: unknown, context?: Record<string, string>): Promise<void> {
  try {
    const Sentry = await import("@sentry/node")
    if (!Sentry.isInitialized()) return
    Sentry.captureException(err, context ? { tags: context } : undefined)
  } catch {}
}

const SELF_ASSESSMENT_MARKER = "## Supervisor Self-Assessment"
const FEEDBACK_MARKER = "## Supervisor:"
// DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS, DEFAULT_MAX_GOAL_DURATION_MS, resolveMaxAttempts now come
// from ../core/goal.mjs (shared with the Claude Code side).

/**
 * Read the top-level `maxAttempts:` integer from ~/.config/opencode/supervisor.yaml.
 * Returns undefined if the file is missing, the key is absent, or the value is invalid.
 */
async function loadConfiguredMaxAttempts(): Promise<number | undefined> {
  try {
    const content = await readFile(SUPERVISOR_CONFIG_PATH, "utf-8")
    for (const rawLine of content.split(/\r?\n/)) {
      const line = rawLine.trim()
      if (!line || line.startsWith("#")) continue
      const match = line.match(/^maxAttempts\s*:\s*(\S+)/)
      if (match) {
        const raw = match[1].trim()
        if (/^\d+$/.test(raw)) {
          const value = parseInt(raw, 10)
          if (Number.isFinite(value)) return value
        }
      }
    }
  } catch {}
  return undefined
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Supervisor per-session persistence (supervisorStore)
//
// Stores goal + retry state at <directory>/.supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json
// so supervisor decisions survive across tool invocations within a session.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface SupervisorGoal {
  condition: string
  status: "active" | "paused" | "achieved" | "cleared" | "exhausted"
  attempts: number
  tokenBaseline: number
  startedAt: number
  deadline: number
  lastReason: string
}

export interface SupervisorState {
  maxAttempts?: number       // /supervisor:retry override (optional)
  goal?: SupervisorGoal
}

function supervisorDir(directory: string): string {
  return join(directory, ".supervisor", "goals")
}

/** Guard against path-traversal attacks via a malformed sessionId. */
function assertSafeSessionId(sessionId: string): void {
  if (!sessionId || sessionId.includes("/") || sessionId.includes("\\") || sessionId.includes("..")) {
    throw new Error(`Invalid sessionId: ${JSON.stringify(sessionId)}`)
  }
}

function supervisorPath(directory: string, sessionId: string): string {
  return join(supervisorDir(directory), `${sessionId}.json`)
}

export const supervisorStore = {
  async load(directory: string, sessionId: string): Promise<SupervisorState> {
    try {
      const content = await readFile(supervisorPath(directory, sessionId), "utf-8")
      return JSON.parse(content) as SupervisorState
    } catch {
      return {}
    }
  },

  async save(directory: string, sessionId: string, state: SupervisorState): Promise<void> {
    assertSafeSessionId(sessionId)
    const dir = supervisorDir(directory)
    await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true })
    const filePath = supervisorPath(directory, sessionId)
    await writeFile(filePath, JSON.stringify(state, null, 2), { mode: 0o600 })
    await chmod(filePath, 0o600)
  },

  async setGoal(
    directory: string,
    sessionId: string,
    condition: string,
    opts?: { tokenBaseline?: number; now?: number; maxDurationMs?: number }

/**
 * on-stop.mjs — Claude Code Stop hook entry-point for the supervisor plugin.
 *
 * Reads the CC Stop payload from stdin (JSON), applies safety rails, reads the
 * transcript tail, builds a stop-context, classifies it with the shared judge

 * changes.

 *   writeAttemptCounter, writeVerdict, sanitizeCwd, debug
 */

import path from 'node:path';

const TRANSCRIPT_MAX_BYTES = 200_000;
const TRANSCRIPT_MAX_ENTRIES = 50;

process.on('uncaughtException', (err) => {
  _debugRaw({ msg: 'uncaught_exception', error: String(err), stack: err?.stack });
  process.exit(0);
});
process.on('unhandledRejection', (reason) => {
  _debugRaw({ msg: 'unhandled_rejection', reason: String(reason) });
  process.exit(0);
});

function _debugRaw(obj) {
  if (!DEBUG_ENABLED) return;
  const line = JSON.stringify({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), ...obj }) + '\n';
  try {
    fs.appendFileSync('/tmp/supervisor-cc-crash.log', line);
  } catch {
    process.stderr.write(line);
  }
}

export function debug(obj, cwd) {
  if (!DEBUG_ENABLED) return;
  const dir = path.join(cwd ?? process.cwd(), STATE_DIR);
  const line = JSON.stringify({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), ...obj }) + '\n';
  try {
    fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    fs.appendFileSync(path.join(dir, 'debug.log'), line);
  } catch (err) {
    process.stderr.write(`[on-stop.mjs] debug write failed: ${err}\n`);
  }
}

export function sanitizeCwd(cwd) {
  if (typeof cwd !== 'string' || !path.isAbsolute(cwd)) {
    throw new Error('supervisor: invalid cwd');
  }
  const resolved = path.resolve(cwd);
  if (resolved !== cwd) throw new Error('supervisor: invalid cwd');
  return resolved;
}

export function loopGuard(stopPayload) {
  return stopPayload?.stop_hook_active === true;
}

  const file = path.join(cwd, STATE_DIR, `${session_id}_attempts.json`);

<sub>⏱️ 509m 21s</sub>

---

# How We Keep AI Agents From Stopping Too Early

Most AI coding agents have the same failure mode: they stop mid-task and ask you what to do next, or write "next steps" and call it done. Here's how we handle that in our opencode setup — using opencode's built-in Autopilot mode, a `/goal` plugin, and a lightweight supervisor that watches every session.

---

## The Problem

You ask an agent to "write the feature, add tests, open a PR." It writes the feature. Then stops and says:

\> "Next steps: write the tests and open the PR."

That's not done. That's a handoff disguised as completion. You have to re-prompt. Multiply that by 10 sessions a day and it's a significant tax.

---

## Three Layers We Use

### 1. Autopilot Mode (built into opencode)

opencode has a built-in agent named `autopilot`. It's a variant of `build` mode with one key difference: the agent is **not allowed to stop** on its own. To exit, it must call a special tool — `autopilot_exit` — which signals "I'm done, here's the summary."

```
User prompt
    │
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────┐
│      autopilot agent        │
│                             │
│  has: question, plan_enter  │
│  has: autopilot_exit ✓      │
│  denied: normal stop        │
│                             │
│  [runs tools until done]    │
│         │                   │
│         ▼                   │
│   autopilot_exit()          │
└────────────┬────────────────┘
             │
             ▼
       build agent
   "Briefly summarize
    what was accomplished."
```

The `autopilot_exit` description tells the model:

\> "Call this tool only after the implementation is complete. Do NOT call it if you still need to run more tools."

That instruction alone catches a lot of premature exits — the model has to explicitly choose to stop, and the description makes the bar clear.

**How to use it:** Switch to autopilot from the agent picker in the TUI, or set `agent: autopilot` in your session config.

---

### 2. The Supervisor Plugin — Catches What Autopilot Misses

Autopilot handles sessions where you've deliberately enabled autonomous mode. But most sessions are normal `build` mode, where the agent *can* stop whenever it wants.

The supervisor plugin hooks into every `session.idle` event. When the agent goes quiet, the supervisor calls a small LLM judge with the last N messages and asks: *did this agent stop legitimately, or too early?*

The judge classifies each stop into one of six categories:

```
Agent stops
    │
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Judge (claude-haiku)            │
│                                             │
│  complete                → leave alone      │
│  waiting_for_user        → leave alone      │
│  tool_available_punt     → re-prompt  ──┐   │
│  summary_drift_stop      → re-prompt  ──┤   │
│  genuinely_stuck         → re-prompt  ──┘   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
             │
             ▼
     Continuation prompt injected
     (escalating over up to 3 attempts)
```

`tool_available_punt`: agent had Bash/browser tools but asked the user to run something instead.

`summary_drift_stop`: agent wrote "Next: run the tests" then stopped without running them.

`genuinely_stuck`: agent just... stopped. Short response, no question, no summary.

These three patterns get a nudge. The other two don't.

The patterns (category descriptions + anti-patterns + few-shot examples) live in `core/patterns.json`. Running `/supervisor:train` mines your own session history — "agent stopped, then user had to follow up" pairs — and updates your local patterns file. Your sessions sharpen the judge without touching any shared code.

---

### 3. The `/goal` Command — Guaranteed Completion

Sometimes "don't stop early" isn't enough. You want the agent to keep going **until a specific condition is true** — tests passing, CI green, PR open.

```
/supervisor:goal all tests in test/auth pass and the PR is open with green CI
```

This sets an active goal stored at `.supervisor/goals/\<sessionId\>.json`. On every `session.idle`, the supervisor checks the goal:

```
session.idle fires
    │
    ▼
Is a goal active?
    │
    ├── No  →  normal supervisor classification
    │
    └── Yes →  Is goal condition demonstrably met?
                    │
                    ├── Yes  →  let agent stop, mark goal achieved
                    │
                    └── No   →  inject continuation prompt
                                (up to 16 attempts before exhausting)
```

The goal text is injected as a **mandatory completion requirement** in the judge's prompt. The agent sees it on every continuation — it knows it cannot stop until the condition is met.

```bash
/supervisor:goal              # check current goal + attempts
/supervisor:retry 24          # raise the attempt budget
/supervisor:goal clear        # cancel the goal
```

State is per-session, mode 0600, never committed to git.

---

## How the Three Layers Fit Together

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   opencode session                   │
│                                                      │
│  autopilot mode ──────► explicit exit required       │
│                                                      │
│  build mode + supervisor:                            │
│    session.idle ──► judge ──► re-prompt (≤3x)        │
│                                                      │
│  goal active:                                        │
│    session.idle ──► goal check ──► re-prompt (≤16x)  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The same supervisor also runs inside **Claude Code** via its `Stop` hook — the same judge, same patterns file, same goal logic. One config file (`core/patterns.json`), two runtimes.

---

## Trade-offs

**More re-prompts = more tokens.** The supervisor adds one small LLM call (haiku-class, 250 tokens max) per idle event. On a busy session that's maybe 5–10 extra calls. Worthwhile if it saves you 3 manual re-prompts.

**False positives are cheap to fix.** If the supervisor nudges an agent that was legitimately done, the agent just responds "I've already completed that." `/supervisor:train` will learn from that session and downweight the false positive pattern.

**Goal mode is aggressive.** With a 16-attempt budget the agent will keep going even if it's spinning. Set a realistic goal condition and watch the first couple of sessions.

---

## Setup

For **opencode**, add to `opencode.json`:

```json
{ "plugin": ["opencode-supervisor"] }
```

For **Claude Code**, from your terminal:

```bash
claude plugin marketplace add dzianisv/agents-supervisor
claude plugin install supervisor@agents-supervisor
```

The supervisor is active in the next session. Source at [github.com/dzianisv/agents-supervisor](https://github.com/dzianisv/agents-supervisor).

### ✅ `bash`

**Read goal.mjs source of truth**

$ cat ~/workspace/agents-supervisor/core/goal.mjs

<details>
<summary>116 lines</summary>

```
/**
 * goal.mjs — shared goal-loop primitives (canonical JS port of the logic in
 * opencode/supervisor.ts). Used by the Claude Code Stop hook (bin/on-stop.mjs)
 * and the CC goal/retry CLI (bin/supervisor-goal.mjs).
 *
 * A "goal" is a user-set condition the agent must demonstrably meet before the
 * supervisor lets it stop. While a goal is active the retry budget rises
 * (default 16) and the goal is injected into the judge prompt as a mandatory
 * completion requirement.
 *
 * State: <cwd>/.supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json (mode 0600).
 */

import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';

export const DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 16;
export const DEFAULT_MAX_GOAL_DURATION_MS = 30 * 60 * 1000;

const GOAL_CLEAR_ALIASES = new Set(['clear', 'stop', 'off', 'reset', 'none', 'cancel']);

/** Priority: sessionOverride > config > 16. Clamped [1,100]; non-finite ignored. */
export function resolveMaxAttempts(opts = {}) {
  for (const c of [opts.sessionOverride, opts.config, DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS]) {
    if (c !== undefined && Number.isFinite(c)) return Math.min(100, Math.max(1, Math.floor(c)));
  }
  return DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS;
}

/** Parse `/supervisor:goal`/`/supervisor:retry` args into a typed action. */
export function parseSupervisorCommand(name, args) {
  const trimmed = (args ?? '').trim();
  if (name === 'goal') {
    if (trimmed === '') return { kind: 'goal-status' };
    if (GOAL_CLEAR_ALIASES.has(trimmed.toLowerCase())) return { kind: 'goal-clear' };
    return { kind: 'goal-set', condition: trimmed.slice(0, 4000) };
  }
  if (name === 'retry') {
    if (trimmed === '') return { kind: 'retry-status' };
    if (/^\d+$/.test(trimmed)) return { kind: 'retry-set', n: parseInt(trimmed, 10) };
    return { kind: 'retry-status' };
  }
  return { kind: 'unknown', name };
}

/** Prompt fragment marking the goal a MANDATORY completion requirement (or '' if blank). */
export function buildGoalRequirementSection(condition) {
  const trimmed = (condition ?? '').trim();
  if (!trimmed) return '';
  return `## GOAL (mandatory completion requirement)

MANDATORY: The following user-set goal condition MUST be demonstrably met before the task can be considered complete:

  "${trimmed}"

This is an ADDITIONAL requirement on top of the existing workflow gates (tests, build, PR, CI checks) — it does not replace them. The task is NOT complete until this condition is satisfied AND all normal completion criteria are met.

Evidence rule: a claim that this goal condition is met must be backed by evidence already surfaced in the transcript — commands run and their output, tests actually executed, artefacts produced. A bare assertion that the condition is satisfied does NOT count as evidence.`;
}

/**
 * Decide the next goal state. Strict order: exhausted (attempts cap OR deadline)
 * → achieved (complete) → continue (attempts+1). Returns a NEW goal; never mutates.
 */
export function decideGoalTransition({ goal, complete, now, maxAttempts, reason }) {
  const lastReason = reason ?? goal.lastReason;
  if (goal.attempts >= maxAttempts || now >= goal.deadline) {
    return { action: 'exhausted', goal: { ...goal, status: 'exhausted', lastReason } };
  }
  if (complete) {
    return { action: 'achieved', goal: { ...goal, status: 'achieved', lastReason } };
  }
  return { action: 'continue', goal: { ...goal, status: 'active', attempts: goal.attempts + 1, lastReason } };
}

// --- per-session store (sync) ---------------------------------------------

function assertSafeSessionId(sessionId) {
  if (!sessionId || sessionId.includes('/') || sessionId.includes('\\') || sessionId.includes('..')) {
    throw new Error(`Invalid sessionId: ${JSON.stringify(sessionId)}`);
  }
}
function goalsDir(cwd) {
  return path.join(cwd, '.supervisor', 'goals');
}
function goalPath(cwd, sessionId) {
  return path.join(goalsDir(cwd), `${sessionId}.json`);
}

export const goalStore = {
  load(cwd, sessionId) {
    try {
      return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(goalPath(cwd, sessionId), 'utf8'));
    } catch {
      return {};
    }
  },
  save(cwd, sessionId, state) {
    assertSafeSessionId(sessionId);
    fs.mkdirSync(goalsDir(cwd), { recursive: true });
    const p = goalPath(cwd, sessionId);
    fs.writeFileSync(p, JSON.stringify(state, null, 2), { mode: 0o600 });
    try { fs.chmodSync(p, 0o600); } catch { /* best effort */ }
  },
  setGoal(cwd, sessionId, condition, opts = {}) {
    const state = goalStore.load(cwd, sessionId);
    const now = opts.now ?? Date.now();
    const maxDurationMs = opts.maxDurationMs ?? DEFAULT_MAX_GOAL_DURATION_MS;
    state.goal = {
      condition, status: 'active', attempts: 0,
      tokenBaseline: opts.tokenBaseline ?? 0, startedAt: now,
      deadline: now + maxDurationMs, lastReason: '',
    };
    goalStore.save(cwd, sessionId, state);
    return state;
  },
  clearGoal(cwd, sessionId) {
    const state = goalStore.load(cwd, sessionId);
    delete state.goal;
    goalStore.save(cwd, sessionId, state);
    return state;
  },
  setRetry(cwd, sessionId, n) {
    const state = goalStore.load(cwd, sessionId);
    state.maxAttempts = n;
    goalStore.save(cwd, sessionId, state);
    return state;
  },
};

          lastReflectedMsgId.set(sessionId, lastUserMsgId)
          return
        }

        // session.idle (triggered by the agent responding to feedback) does not
        // start another supervisor cycle for the same user message.

        // so goal.attempts stays unchanged — no silent budget loss.

        activeSupervisors.delete(sessionId)
      }

    config: async (_config) => {
      return
    },
    tool: (setSupervisorTool || supervisorToggleTool || supervisorTrainTool || supervisorGoalTool || supervisorRetryTool) ? {
      ...(setSupervisorTool ? { set_supervisor: setSupervisorTool } : {}),

    event: async ({ event }: { event: { type: string; properties?: any } }) => {
      debug("event received:", event.type)
      if (event.type === "session.error") {
        const props = (event as any).properties
        const sessionId = props?.sessionID
        const error = props?.error
        if (sessionId && error?.name === "MessageAbortedError") {
          recentlyAbortedSessions.set(sessionId, Date.now())
          debug("Session aborted (Esc), cooldown started:", sessionId.slice(0, 8))
        }
      }

      if (event.type === "session.idle") {
        debug("session.idle received")
        const sessionId = (event as any).properties?.sessionID
        if (!sessionId || typeof sessionId !== "string") return

        const abortTime = recentlyAbortedSessions.get(sessionId)
        if (abortTime) {
          const elapsed = Date.now() - abortTime
          if (elapsed < ABORT_COOLDOWN) return
          recentlyAbortedSessions.delete(sessionId)
        }

        // Issue #109: Brief delay before running supervisor to let session.error
        // arrive first (handles race where session.idle fires before session.error
        // when user presses ESC). After the delay, re-check the abort map.
        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, ABORT_RACE_DELAY))
        const postDelayAbortTime = recentlyAbortedSessions.get(sessionId)
        if (postDelayAbortTime) {
          debug("Session aborted during race delay, skipping supervisor:", sessionId.slice(0, 8))
          return
        }

        try {
          await runSupervisor(sessionId)
        } catch (e: any) {
          debug("runSupervisor error:", e?.message || e)
          reportError(e, { plugin: "supervisor-3", op: "run-supervisor" })
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

async function analyzeSelfAssessmentWithLLM(
  client: any,
  directory: string,
  context: TaskContext,
  selfAssessment: string,
  judgeSessionIds: Set<string>,
  toolSupervisorPrompt?: string | null,
  rubric?: Rubric
): Promise<SupervisorAnalysis | null> {
  const modelList = await loadSupervisorModelList()
  const preferredModel = await loadPreferredModelSpec(directory)
  const attempts = modelList.length
    ? modelList
    : preferredModel && !isBlockedJudgeModel(preferredModel)
      ? [preferredModel]
      : [""]

  const prompt = buildJudgePrompt(context, selfAssessment, toolSupervisorPrompt, rubric)

  for (const modelSpec of attempts) {
    let judgeSession: any
    try {
      const { data } = await client.session.create({ query: { directory } })
      judgeSession = data
    } catch {
      return null
    }
    if (!judgeSession?.id) return null
    judgeSessionIds.add(judgeSession.id)

    try {
      const modelParts = modelSpec ? modelSpec.split("/") : []
      const providerID = modelParts[0] || ""
      const modelID = modelParts.slice(1).join("/") || ""

      const body: any = { parts: [{ type: "text", text: prompt }] }
      if (providerID && modelID) body.model = { providerID, modelID }

      await client.session.promptAsync({
        path: { id: judgeSession.id },
        body
      })

      const response = await waitForResponse(client, judgeSession.id)
      if (!response) continue

      const jsonMatch = response.match(/\{[\s\S]*\}/)
      if (!jsonMatch) continue

      const verdict = JSON.parse(jsonMatch[0]) as any
      const missing = Array.isArray(verdict.missing) ? verdict.missing : []
      const humanOnlyMissing = missing.filter((item: string) => isHumanOnlyAction(item))
      const actionableMissing = missing.filter((item: string) => !isHumanOnlyAction(item))
      const actionableNextActions = Array.isArray(verdict.next_actions)
        ? verdict.next_actions.filter((item: string) => typeof item === "string" && !isHumanOnlyAction(item))
        : []
      const shouldContinue = !verdict.complete && (actionableMissing.length > 0 || actionableNextActions.length > 0)
      const requiresHumanAction = !!verdict.requires_human_action || humanOnlyMissing.length > 0
      return {
        complete: !!verdict.complete,
        shouldContinue,
        reason: verdict.feedback || "Judge analysis completed",
        missing,
        nextActions: Array.isArray(verdict.next_actions) ? verdict.next_actions : [],
        requiresHumanAction,
        severity: verdict.severity || "MEDIUM"
      }
    } catch (e) {
      reportError(e, { plugin: "supervisor-3", op: "judge-session" })

```
async function runSupervisor(sessionId: string): Promise<void> {
      debug("runSupervisor called for session:", sessionId.slice(0, 8))
      if (activeSupervisors.has(sessionId)) return

      currentSessionId = sessionId
      if (await isSessionDisabled(sessionId)) {
        debug("Supervisor disabled for session:", sessionId.slice(0, 8))
        return
      }

      activeSupervisors.add(sessionId)

      try {
        let messages: any[] | undefined
        try {
          const { data } = await client.session.messages({ path: { id: sessionId } })
          messages = Array.isArray(data) ? data : undefined
        } catch {
          debug("Session not found (likely deleted), skipping supervisor:", sessionId.slice(0, 8))
          return
        }
        if (!messages || messages.length < 2) return

        if (isJudgeSession(sessionId, messages, judgeSessionIds)) return
        if (isPlanMode(messages)) return

        // Issue #82 / Issue #109: ESC-abort detection.
        // Previously we checked time.completed on the last assistant message, but
        // that caused false negatives — sessions where the model stopped producing
        // output (context exhaustion, rate limit, etc.) also lack time.completed,
        // and supervisor would silently skip them.
        // We now rely solely on the recentlyAbortedSessions map (populated by
        // session.error with MessageAbortedError) plus a brief delay in the event
        // handler to handle the race where session.idle fires before session.error.

        const lastUserMsgId = getLastRelevantUserMessageId(messages)
        if (!lastUserMsgId) return

        const initialUserMsgId = lastUserMsgId
        const attemptKey = `${sessionId}:${lastUserMsgId}`
        const lastReflectedId = lastReflectedMsgId.get(sessionId)
        if (lastUserMsgId === lastReflectedId) return

        const context = await buildTaskContext(messages, directory)
        if (!context) return

        const lastAssistantText = extractLastAssistantText(messages)
        const customPrompt = await loadSupervisorPrompt(directory)
        const agents = await getAgentsFile(directory)
        const rubric = await loadRubric(directory)
        const currentAttemptCount = attempts.get(attemptKey) || 0
        // Supervisor goal/retry state. A /supervisor:retry override applies even
        // without a goal (sessionOverride). An active goal augments the judge
        // rubric with a mandatory completion requirement and drives the goal loop.
        const sup = await supervisorStore.load(directory, sessionId)
        const goal = sup.goal && sup.goal.status === "active" ? sup.goal : null
        const effectiveMaxAttempts = resolveMaxAttempts({ sessionOverride: sup.maxAttempts, config: await loadConfiguredMaxAttempts() })
        // When a goal is active, its condition is the strongest stated intent and
        // MUST compose with the existing gates: build the DEFAULT rubric prompt
        // with the goal requirement appended, bypassing file/tool precedence.
        const goalSection = goal ? buildGoalRequirementSection(goal.condition) : ""
        const effRubric: Rubric = goalSection
          ? { ...rubric, antipatterns: `${rubric.antipatterns}\n\n${goalSection}` }
          : rubric
        const defaultSupervisorPrompt = buildSelfAssessmentPrompt(
          context,
          agents,
          lastAssistantText,
          currentAttemptCount,
          effRubric,
          effectiveMaxAttempts
        )
        let supervisorPrompt: string
        let effectiveToolSupervisorPrompt: string | null
        let promptSource: "file" | "tool" | "default" | "goal"
        if (goal) {
          // Goal active: use the default (goal-augmented) prompt directly.
          supervisorPrompt = defaultSupervisorPrompt
          effectiveToolSupervisorPrompt = null
          promptSource = "goal"
        } else {
          const resolvedPrompt = resolveSupervisorPromptPrecedence(customPrompt, toolSupervisorPrompt, defaultSupervisorPrompt)
          supervisorPrompt = resolvedPrompt.prompt
          effectiveToolSupervisorPrompt = resolvedPrompt.effectiveToolSupervisorPrompt
          promptSource = resolvedPrompt.source
        }

        await showToast(client, directory, "Requesting supervisor self-assessment...", "info")
        debug("Requesting supervisor self-assessment (source:", promptSource, ")")

        // Issue #98: Run self-assessment in a separate ephemeral session instead
        // of prompting the active agent session. Asking the active session to
        // respond in JSON poisons its context, causing subsequent replies to be
        // JSON-only.
        const modelList = await loadSupervisorModelList()
        const preferredModel = await loadPreferredModelSpec(directory)
        const assessmentAttempts = modelList.length
          ? modelList
          : preferredModel && !isBlockedJudgeModel(preferredModel)
            ? [preferredModel]
            : [""]

      let selfAssessment: string | null = null
      let assessmentModelSpec: string | null = null
      for (const modelSpec of assessmentAttempts) {
        let assessmentSession: any
        try {
          const { data } = await client.session.create({ query: { directory } })
            assessmentSession = data
          } catch {
            debug("Failed to create self-assessment session")
            lastReflectedMsgId.set(sessionId, lastUserMsgId)
            return
          }
          if (!assessmentSession?.id) {
            lastReflectedMsgId.set(sessionId, lastUserMsgId)
<shellId: 66 completed with exit code 0>

Key things I learned:

### ✅ `bash`

$ cat /Users/engineer/workspace/opencode/packages/opencode/src/tool/autopilot.ts

<details>
<summary>65 lines</summary>

```
import { Effect, Schema } from "effect"
import { Session } from "@/session/session"
import { MessageV2 } from "../session/message-v2"
import { Provider } from "@/provider/provider"
import { Database } from "@opencode-ai/core/database/database"
import { type SessionID, MessageID, PartID } from "../session/schema"
import * as Tool from "./tool"
import DESCRIPTION from "./autopilot-exit.txt"

function getLastModel(sessionID: SessionID) {
  return Effect.gen(function* () {
    const messages = yield* MessageV2.stream(sessionID)
    for (const item of messages) {
      if (item.info.role === "user" && item.info.model) return item.info.model
    }
    return undefined
  })
}

export const Parameters = Schema.Struct({})

export const AutopilotExitTool = Tool.define(
  "autopilot_exit",
  Effect.gen(function* () {
    const session = yield* Session.Service
    const provider = yield* Provider.Service
    const database = yield* Database.Service

    return {
      description: DESCRIPTION,
      parameters: Parameters,
      execute: (_params: {}, ctx: Tool.Context) =>
        Effect.gen(function* () {
          yield* ctx.ask({
            permission: "autopilot_exit",
            patterns: ["*"],
            always: ["*"],
            metadata: {},
          })

          const model =
            (yield* getLastModel(ctx.sessionID).pipe(Effect.provideService(Database.Service, database))) ??
            (yield* provider.defaultModel())
          const msg: MessageV2.User = {
            id: MessageID.ascending(),
            sessionID: ctx.sessionID,
            role: "user",
            time: { created: Date.now() },
            agent: "build",
            model,
          }
          yield* session.updateMessage(msg)
          yield* session.updatePart({
            id: PartID.ascending(),
            messageID: msg.id,
            sessionID: ctx.sessionID,
            type: "text",
            text: "Autopilot completed. Briefly summarize what was accomplished.",
            synthetic: true,
          } satisfies MessageV2.TextPart)

          return {
            title: "Exited autopilot",
            output: "Autopilot completed and switched to build for a summary.",
            metadata: {},
          }
        }).pipe(Effect.orDie),
    }
  }),
)

```
export function resolveMaxAttempts(opts = {}) {
  for (const c of [opts.sessionOverride, opts.config, DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS]) {
    if (c !== undefined && Number.isFinite(c)) return Math.min(100, Math.max(1, Math.floor(c)));
  }
  return DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS;
}

/** Parse `/supervisor:goal`/`/supervisor:retry` args into a typed action. */
export function parseSupervisorCommand(name, args) {
  const trimmed = (args ?? '').trim();
  if (name === 'goal') {
    if (trimmed === '') return { kind: 'goal-status' };
    if (GOAL_CLEAR_ALIASES.has(trimmed.toLowerCase())) return { kind: 'goal-clear' };
    return { kind: 'goal-set', condition: trimmed.slice(0, 4000) };
  }
  if (name === 'retry') {
    if (trimmed === '') return { kind: 'retry-status' };
    if (/^\d+$/.test(trimmed)) return { kind: 'retry-set', n: parseInt(trimmed, 10) };
    return { kind: 'retry-status' };
  }
  return { kind: 'unknown', name };
}

/** Prompt fragment marking the goal a MANDATORY completion requirement (or '' if blank). */
export function buildGoalRequirementSection(condition) {
  const trimmed = (condition ?? '').trim();
  if (!trimmed) return '';
  return `## GOAL (mandatory completion requirement)

MANDATORY: The following user-set goal condition MUST be demonstrably met before the task can be considered complete:

--
export function decideGoalTransition({ goal, complete, now, maxAttempts, reason }) {
  const lastReason = reason ?? goal.lastReason;
  if (goal.attempts >= maxAttempts || now >= goal.deadline) {
    return { action: 'exhausted', goal: { ...goal, status: 'exhausted', lastReason } };
  }
  if (complete) {
    return { action: 'achieved', goal: { ...goal, status: 'achieved', lastReason } };
  }
  return { action: 'continue', goal: { ...goal, status: 'active', attempts: goal.attempts + 1, lastReason } };
}

// --- per-session store (sync) ---------------------------------------------

function assertSafeSessionId(sessionId) {
  if (!sessionId || sessionId.includes('/') || sessionId.includes('\\') || sessionId.includes('..')) {
    throw new Error(`Invalid sessionId: ${JSON.stringify(sessionId)}`);
  }
}
function goalsDir(cwd) {
  return path.join(cwd, '.supervisor', 'goals');
}
function goalPath(cwd, sessionId) {
  return path.join(goalsDir(cwd), `${sessionId}.json`);
}

export const goalStore = {
  load(cwd, sessionId) {
    try {
      return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(goalPath(cwd, sessionId), 'utf8'));
    } catch {
      return {};

<sub>⏱️ 535m 15s</sub>

### ✅ `bash`

**Rewrite blog post with detailed flow diagrams for all 3 layers**

$ cat > /Users/engineer/workspace/blogposts/3ways-to-push-ai.md << 'BLOGPOST'
# 3 Ways to Stop AI Agents From Quitting Too Early

*You ask the agent to write a feature, add tests, and open a PR. It writes the feature. Then it stops.*

> "Next steps: add unit tests, update the README, open a pull request."

That's not done. That's a handoff disguised as completion. You re-prompt. It does the next bit. Stops again. Multiply this across a day and you've spent 30 minutes babysitting a loop that should have run unattended.

We've built three layers on top of [opencode](https://github.com/dzianisv/opencode) to handle this. Each targets a different cause of premature stopping.

---

## Layer 1: Autopilot Mode

**Problem:** In normal build mode the agent can stop whenever it decides to. There's no structural mechanism requiring it to finish.

**What we built:** An `autopilot` agent in our [opencode fork](https://github.com/dzianisv/opencode) that has **no normal exit path**. The only way out is calling `autopilot_exit`.

The tool's description (which the model reads on every turn):
> "Call this tool only after the implementation is complete. Do NOT call it if you still need to run more tools to finish the task."

```
User sends prompt in autopilot mode
             │
             ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                  autopilot agent                  │
  │                                                   │
  │  All tools available:                             │
  │    read/write/shell/search/browser/...            │
  │                                                   │
  │  ┌── turn 1 ──────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │  │  runs tools, produces output               │  │
  │  │  NO tool called "autopilot_exit"           │  │
  │  │           → forced to continue             │  │
  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
  │                      │                            │
  │                      ▼                            │
  │  ┌── turn 2 ──────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │  │  runs more tools                           │  │
  │  │  still not calling autopilot_exit          │  │
  │  │           → still forced to continue       │  │
  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
  │                      │                            │
  │                      ▼                            │
  │  ┌── turn N ──────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │  │  work is done                              │  │
  │  │  calls autopilot_exit()         ◄──────────┼──┼── ONLY exit
  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
  └────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
  autopilot_exit() executes:
    1. Injects synthetic user message:
       "Autopilot completed. Briefly summarize
        what was accomplished."
    2. Switches agent context to: "build"
                       │
                       ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                  build agent                      │
  │  Reads synthetic message, writes summary,         │
  │  returns control to user.                         │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**When to use it:** Well-scoped task, you want to walk away. Not great for open-ended exploration where you expect genuine back-and-forth.

---

## Layer 2: The Supervisor Plugin

**Problem:** Autopilot requires opting in. Most sessions are still normal build mode, and the agent can stop at any phrase like "Here's what I've done so far" or "You can now run X."

**What we built:** The [agents-supervisor](https://github.com/dzianisv/agents-supervisor) plugin hooks into every `session.idle` event. When the agent goes quiet, it doesn't just classify the last message — it runs **two separate LLM calls**.

```
Agent goes idle (session.idle fires)
             │
             ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                 runSupervisor()                   │
  │                                                   │
  │  1. Fetch session message history                 │
  │  2. Build task context:                           │
  │       taskType, requiresTests, requiresPR,        │
  │       requiresCI, requiresBuild, ...              │
  │  3. Check: is this a judge session itself?        │
  │     (prevents infinite loops) → skip if yes      │
  │  4. Check: same user message as last time?        │
  │     → skip if already reflected                  │
  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Step 1: Self-Assessment                   │
  │                                                   │
  │  Spin up ephemeral session A (throwaway)          │
  │                                                   │
  │  Send it the full task context + prompt:          │
  │  "Is the work done? What's missing?               │
  │   What are the next concrete actions?"            │
  │                                                   │
  │  ← returns JSON:                                  │
  │    {                                              │
  │      "complete": false,                           │
  │      "missing": ["tests not run", ...],           │
  │      "next_actions": ["run bun test", ...],       │
  │      "requires_human_action": false               │
  │    }                                              │
  │                                                   │
  │  ⚠ Why ephemeral? Asking the ACTIVE session to   │
  │    respond in JSON corrupts its context.          │
  │    Every subsequent reply drifts to JSON-only.    │
  │    Throwaway session keeps the active one clean.  │
  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Step 2: Judge                             │
  │                                                   │
  │  Spin up ephemeral session B (throwaway)          │
  │                                                   │
  │  Send it: self-assessment JSON + patterns rubric  │
  │                                                   │
  │  Patterns (from core/patterns.json):              │
  │    tool_available_punt:                           │
  │      agent asked user to run something            │
  │      the agent's own tools could handle           │
  │    summary_drift_stop:                            │
  │      wrote "Next steps: X" and stopped            │
  │      before doing X                              │
  │    genuinely_stuck:                               │
  │      short reply, no question, no conclusion     │
  │                                                   │
  │  ← returns verdict:                               │
  │    { "complete": false, "severity": "HIGH",       │
  │      "feedback": "...", "next_actions": [...] }   │
  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                         │
              ┌──────────┴──────────┐
              │                     │
         complete=true         complete=false
         (or human-only gap)   + actionable steps
              │                     │
              ▼                     ▼
         leave alone          inject continuation
                              prompt into original
                              session (≤ 3 attempts)
                                     │
                                     ▼
                              agent wakes up,
                              reads feedback,
                              continues work
```

**Learning from your own data:** `/supervisor:train` mines your past sessions — specifically "agent stopped, user had to re-prompt" pairs — and writes a user-local patterns file that overrides the defaults. The judge gets sharper over time from your own history.

```
                 ┌────────────────────┐
                 │  core/patterns.json │  ← shared default rules
                 └────────┬───────────┘
                          │  merged with
                 ┌────────┴───────────┐
                 │  ~/.supervisor/     │  ← user-local patterns
                 │  patterns.json      │    (from /supervisor:train)
                 └────────┬───────────┘
                          │
              ┌───────────┴────────────┐
              │                        │
              ▼                        ▼
   ┌─────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────┐
   │  OpenCode plugin     │   │  Claude Code plugin   │
   │  session.idle hook  │   │  Stop hook            │
   │  supervisor.ts      │   │  bin/on-stop.mjs      │
   └─────────────────────┘   └──────────────────────┘
   same core/goal.mjs logic on both runtimes
```

---

## Layer 3: The `/goal` Command

**Problem:** "Don't stop early" is not the same as "the tests actually pass." The supervisor can keep nudging, but it doesn't know what *done* means for your specific task.

**What we built:** A persistent goal condition attached to a session.

```
/supervisor:goal all tests in test/auth pass and the PR is open with green CI
```

This writes an active goal to `.supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json` (mode 0600, never committed). Every subsequent `session.idle` reads it before anything else.

```
User sets goal condition
             │
             ▼
  .supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json
  {
    "status": "active",
    "condition": "all tests in test/auth pass...",
    "attempts": 0,
    "deadline": <now + 30min>,
    "maxAttempts": 16    ← raised from default 3
  }
             │
             ▼
  session.idle fires (agent stopped)
             │
             ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Is a goal active?                               │
  │                                                  │
  │  Yes → goal condition injected into              │
  │         self-assessment prompt:                  │
  │                                                  │
  │  "## GOAL (mandatory completion requirement)     │
  │   MANDATORY: The following user-set goal         │
  │   condition MUST be demonstrably met...          │
  │   Evidence must already be in the transcript.   │
  │   Bare assertions do not count."                 │
  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
  Self-assessment + Judge run (same two-LLM flow as Layer 2)
                         │
                         ▼
  decideGoalTransition() in core/goal.mjs:
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                                                  │
  │  attempts >= maxAttempts OR now >= deadline?     │
  │    → status = "exhausted"  (stop, notify user)   │
  │                                                  │
  │  judge says complete = true?                     │
  │    → status = "achieved"   (goal met, stop)      │
  │                                                  │
  │  otherwise:                                      │
  │    → status = "active"                           │
  │      attempts += 1                               │
  │      inject continuation prompt                  │
  │      (budget only spent if prompt is sent)       │
  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                         │
              ┌──────────┴──────────┐
              │                     │
         achieved /            active: continue
         exhausted
              │                     │
              ▼                     ▼
         stop cleanly          agent wakes up with
                               feedback + goal reminder
                               → attempts next step
                               → session.idle fires again
                               → loop back to top
```

The "evidence must be in the transcript" constraint matters. Without it, an agent satisfies a goal by writing "I believe the tests pass" rather than actually running them.

```
Goal active: what counts as evidence
─────────────────────────────────────
✓  Tool call output showing test run with exit 0
✓  CI URL in the transcript returning green
✓  PR number from a gh pr create command

✗  "The tests should pass now"
✗  "I've implemented what was needed"
✗  "This satisfies the requirement"
```

**Budget only burns on injection.** If the agent hits an early-return (human-only gap, aborted session, etc.), `goal.attempts` stays unchanged. No silent budget loss from noise.

```bash
/supervisor:goal              # check status, attempt count, last reason
/supervisor:retry 24          # raise the attempt budget for this session
/supervisor:goal clear        # cancel
```

---

## How the Three Layers Compose

```
User task comes in
       │
       ▼
Is this an autopilot session?
       │
  Yes  │  No
       │   └──────────────────────────────────────┐
       ▼                                          ▼
┌──────────────────────────┐        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
│      autopilot agent      │        │         build agent          │
│                           │        │                              │
│  Structural constraint:   │        │  Behavioral constraint:      │
│  no exit except           │        │  supervisor fires on idle    │
│  autopilot_exit()         │        │  (≤ 3 nudges by default)     │
│                           │        │                              │
│  Best for:                │        │  Best for:                   │
│  "do this whole thing"    │        │  everyday use, always-on     │
│  unattended runs          │        │                              │
└──────────────┬────────────┘        └──────────────┬──────────────┘
               │                                    │
               ▼                                    ▼
        agent calls                      Is a /goal active?
        autopilot_exit()                      │
               │                    No        │  Yes
               ▼                    │         │
        synthetic summary           │         ▼
        message injected            │    outcome-based loop:
        → build agent writes        │    evidence required in
          summary, stops            │    transcript, 16 attempts,
                                    │    30-min deadline
                                    │
                                    └──► same supervisor loop,
                                         goal-augmented rubric
```

**Rule of thumb:**
- Day-to-day, just run the supervisor plugin — it handles drift and punt patterns automatically.
- Switch to autopilot mode when the task is well-scoped and you want to hand off completely.
- Use `/supervisor:goal` when you have an objective acceptance criterion (tests pass, PR open, endpoint returns 200) and you want the agent to own reaching it.

---

## Trade-offs

**Supervisor adds tokens.** Two LLM calls per idle. Fast/cheap models (haiku-class), but it adds up on busy sessions. Worth it if it replaces manual re-prompts, which it usually does.

**Goal mode is aggressive.** Sixteen attempts will spend tokens if the agent is stuck. Use it for objective, externally verifiable criteria. Not "make it feel better."

**Autopilot is opt-in.** You have to switch modes intentionally. The supervisor runs transparently in the background on every session without any setup.

---

## Setup

**Autopilot mode** — available in our opencode fork. Switch from the agent picker in the TUI.

**Supervisor plugin** for opencode — add to `opencode.json`:

```json
{
  "plugin": ["opencode-supervisor"]
}
```

For Claude Code:

```bash
claude plugin marketplace add dzianisv/agents-supervisor
claude plugin install supervisor@agents-supervisor
```

Active in the next session.

Sources: [github.com/dzianisv/opencode](https://github.com/dzianisv/opencode) · [github.com/dzianisv/agents-supervisor](https://github.com/dzianisv/agents-supervisor)
