```
const runner = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.runner")(function* (
      sessionID: SessionID,
      onInterrupt: Effect.Effect<MessageV2.WithParts>,
    ) {
      const data = yield* InstanceState.get(state)
      const existing = data.runners.get(sessionID)
      if (existing) return existing
      const next = Runner.make<MessageV2.WithParts>(data.scope, {
        onIdle: Effect.gen(function* () {
          data.runners.delete(sessionID)
          yield* status.set(sessionID, { type: "idle" })
        }),
        onBusy: status.set(sessionID, { type: "busy" }),
        onInterrupt,
        busy: () => {
          throw new Session.BusyError(sessionID)
        },
      })
      data.runners.set(sessionID, next)
      return next
    })

    const assertNotBusy = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.assertNotBusy")(function* (sessionID: SessionID) {
      const data = yield* InstanceState.get(state)
      const existing = data.runners.get(sessionID)
      if (existing?.busy) throw new Session.BusyError(sessionID)
    })

    const cancel = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.cancel")(function* (sessionID: SessionID) {
      const data = yield* InstanceState.get(state)
      const existing = data.runners.get(sessionID)
      if (!existing || !existing.busy) {
        yield* status.set(sessionID, { type: "idle" })
        return
      }
      yield* existing.cancel
    })

    const ensureRunning = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.ensureRunning")(function* (
      sessionID: SessionID,
      onInterrupt: Effect.Effect<MessageV2.WithParts>,
      work: Effect.Effect<MessageV2.WithParts>,
    ) {
      return yield* (yield* runner(sessionID, onInterrupt)).ensureRunning(work)
    })

    const startShell = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.startShell")(function* (
      sessionID: SessionID,
      onInterrupt: Effect.Effect<MessageV2.WithParts>,
      work: Effect.Effect<MessageV2.WithParts>,
      ready?: Latch.Latch,
    ) {
      return yield* (yield* runner(sessionID, onInterrupt)).startShell(work, ready)
    })

    // Reset statuses to idle for any session marked busy/retry without an actively-running
    // runner. Guards against onIdle finalizers failing to fire (interrupted scope, etc.)
    // and leaving clients seeing a session stuck as "working" indefinitely.
    const reconcile = Effect.fn("SessionRunState.reconcile")(function* () {
      const data = yield* InstanceState.get(state)
      const statuses = yield* status.list()
      for (const [sessionID, info] of statuses) {
        if (info.type === "idle") continue
        const existing = data.runners.get(sessionID)
        if (existing?.busy) continue
        yield* Effect.logInfo("reconciling stale session status", { sessionID, status: info.type })
        if (existing) data.runners.delete(sessionID)
        yield* status.set(sessionID, { type: "idle" })
      }
    })

    return Service.of({ assertNotBusy, cancel, ensureRunning, startShell, reconcile })
  }),
)

export const defaultLayer = layer.pipe(Layer.provide(SessionStatus.defaultLayer))

export * as SessionRunState from "./run-state"
<shellId: 77 completed with exit code 0>
```

<sub>⏱️ 550m 47s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I understand the full autopilot flow:

1. In autopilot mode, the `prompt.ts` loop detects when the agent has finished a turn without tool calls (i.e., it would naturally "stop")
2. Instead of breaking out of the loop, it injects a **synthetic `\<system-reminder\>` message** with `PROMPT_AUTOPILOT` text: "Autopilot mode remains active. Continue execution autonomously..."
3. This keeps the agent looping **within the same prompt processing loop** — there's no `session.idle` event fired between turns!
4. The agent only escapes this by calling `autopilot_exit`, which switches the agent to `build` mode and injects the summary message


5. The key insight is that autopilot prevents idle by injecting continuation directly inside the prompt loop, completely bypassing the supervisor plugin — they're independent mechanisms. The supervisor would only trigger if the agent somehow exhausted its reflection cap during an autopilot run.*

### 💬 Copilot

Now I understand the full picture. **Autopilot never emits `session.idle`** — it injects a `\<system-reminder\>` continuation *inside the prompt loop itself*, before the runner goes idle. The supervisor never fires during an autopilot run. These are completely independent mechanisms.

<sub>⏱️ 553m 11s</sub>

### ✅ `bash`

**Rewrite with accurate flow diagrams for all 3 layers**

$ cat > /Users/engineer/workspace/blogposts/3ways-to-push-ai.md << 'BLOGPOST'
# 3 Ways to Stop AI Agents From Quitting Too Early

*You ask the agent to write a feature, add tests, and open a PR. It writes the feature. Then it stops.*

> "Next steps: add unit tests, update the README, open a pull request."

That's not done. Three independent mechanisms handle this. Each targets a different cause.

---

## Layer 1: Autopilot Mode — Internal Loop Continuation

**The problem:** In normal build mode the prompt loop runs one turn and exits. The agent replies, the session goes idle, and you're left re-prompting.

**What autopilot does:** It never lets the loop exit normally.

The core of this lives in `session/prompt.ts`. After every model reply, the loop checks whether the agent stopped without calling a tool. In build mode, that means "we're done, break." In autopilot mode, it means "inject a continuation and loop again":

```
User sends a prompt (autopilot mode)
             │
             ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              prompt.ts inner loop                         │
  │                                                           │
  │  ┌── agent turn ───────────────────────────────────────┐ │
  │  │  model produces output + tool calls                 │ │
  │  │  tools execute                                      │ │
  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
  │                         │                                │
  │                         ▼                                │
  │  Did agent stop without tool calls?                      │
  │       │                                                  │
  │       ├── No ──► agent called autopilot_exit()           │
  │       │          (see below)                             │
  │       │                                                  │
  │       └── Yes ──► inject synthetic user message:         │
  │                    ┌──────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │                    │ <system-reminder>                 │  │
  │                    │ Autopilot mode remains active.    │  │
  │                    │ Continue execution autonomously.  │  │
  │                    │ Only stop when fully complete     │  │
  │                    │ or you call autopilot_exit.       │  │
  │                    │ </system-reminder>                │  │
  │                    └──────────────────────────────────┘  │
  │                         │                                │
  │                         └──► loop back, next turn        │
  │                              (up to 50 reflections)      │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  session.idle is NEVER fired during an autopilot run.
  The supervisor plugin NEVER fires during an autopilot run.
  They are completely independent.
```

The only escape is `autopilot_exit`:

```
  agent calls autopilot_exit()
             │
             ▼
  1. Inject synthetic user message:
     "Autopilot completed. Briefly summarize
      what was accomplished."
  2. Switch agent context to: "build"
             │
             ▼
  build agent reads the synthetic message,
  writes a summary, loop exits normally,
  session goes idle.
```

**What this means in practice:** You switch to autopilot mode, type your task, and walk away. The agent keeps looping internally — no idle events, no re-prompting, no supervisor involvement.

---

## Layer 2: The Supervisor Plugin — Idle-Time Judge

**The problem:** Autopilot is opt-in. In normal build mode the agent can stop at "Here's what I've done so far" or "You can now run X." The session goes idle and stays there.

**What the supervisor does:** Hooks into every `session.idle` event. When the agent goes quiet, it runs two LLM calls in ephemeral throwaway sessions to decide whether to nudge the agent to continue.

```
Build mode: agent finishes a turn
             │
             ▼
  session.idle fires ──────────────────────────────────────┐
             │                                             │
             ▼                                             │
  runSupervisor(sessionId)                                 │
             │                                             │
             ▼                                             │
  Guards:                                                  │
    Is this a judge session itself? → skip (no loops)      │
    Is session disabled? → skip                            │
    Same user message as last time? → skip                 │
    ESC-abort cooldown active? → skip                      │
             │                                             │
             ▼                                             │
  Fetch message history + build task context:             │
    taskType, requiresTests, requiresPR,                  │
    requiresCI, requiresBuild, toolsSummary               │
             │                                             │
             ▼                                             │
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
  │  Step 1: Self-assessment                              │ │
  │                                                       │ │
  │  Spin up ephemeral session A (throwaway)              │ │
  │  Send it: task context + assessment prompt            │ │
  │  "Is the work done? What's missing?                   │ │
  │   What are the next concrete actions?"                │ │
  │                                                       │ │
  │  ← JSON response:                                     │ │
  │    { complete: false,                                 │ │
  │      missing: ["tests not run"],                      │ │
  │      next_actions: ["run bun test"],                  │ │
  │      requires_human_action: false }                   │ │
  │                                                       │ │
  │  ⚠ Why throwaway? Asking the ACTIVE session          │ │
  │    to respond in JSON corrupts its context.           │ │
  │    Every subsequent reply drifts to JSON-only.        │ │
  └──────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘ │
                         │                                   │
                         ▼                                   │
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │  Step 2: Judge                                        │  │
  │                                                       │  │
  │  Spin up ephemeral session B (throwaway)              │  │
  │  Send it: self-assessment + patterns rubric           │  │
  │                                                       │  │
  │  Pattern categories that trigger a nudge:             │  │
  │    tool_available_punt                                │  │
  │      agent asked user to run something its            │  │
  │      own tools could have handled                     │  │
  │    summary_drift_stop                                 │  │
  │      wrote "Next steps: X" then stopped               │  │
  │      without doing X                                  │  │
  │    genuinely_stuck                                    │  │
  │      short reply, no question, no conclusion          │  │
  │                                                       │  │
  │  Pattern categories left alone:                       │  │
  │    complete                                           │  │
  │    waiting_for_user_legitimate (OAuth, 2FA,           │  │
  │      real human decision required)                    │  │
  │                                                       │  │
  │  ← JSON verdict:                                      │  │
  │    { complete: false, severity: "HIGH",               │  │
  │      feedback: "...", next_actions: [...] }           │  │
  └──────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘  │
                         │                                   │
              ┌──────────┴──────────┐                       │
              │                     │                       │
         complete=true         complete=false               │
         (or human-only)       + actionable steps           │
              │                     │                       │
              ▼                     ▼                       │
         leave alone          inject feedback prompt        │
         session stays idle   into the original session     │
                                     │                      │
                                     ▼                      │
                              agent wakes up,               │
                              reads feedback,               │
                              does the missing work,        │
                              finishes turn                 │
                                     │                      │
                                     └──────────────────────┘
                                     session.idle fires again
                                     supervisor runs again
                                     (up to 3 attempts by default)
```

**Pattern learning:** `/supervisor:train` mines your past sessions — "agent stopped, you re-prompted" pairs — and writes a user-local patterns file that overrides the defaults. Your own history sharpens the judge.

---

## Layer 3: The `/goal` Command — Outcome-Locked Loop

**The problem:** The supervisor nudges based on patterns. But it doesn't know what *done* means for your task. "All tests pass and CI is green" is not detectable from patterns alone.

**What `/goal` does:** Attaches an acceptance criterion to the session. Every idle event drives toward that criterion specifically.

```
/supervisor:goal all tests in test/auth pass and PR is open with green CI
```

Writes `.supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json`:

```json
{
  "status": "active",
  "condition": "all tests in test/auth pass...",
  "attempts": 0,
  "deadline": "<now + 30 minutes>",
  "maxAttempts": 16
}
```

Every `session.idle` now runs through:

```
session.idle fires
             │
             ▼
  Read goal from .supervisor/goals/<sessionId>.json
             │
             ├── No active goal ──► normal supervisor flow
             │                      (3-attempt budget, pattern-based)
             │
             └── Active goal ──► inject goal into self-assessment prompt:
                                  ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
                                  │ ## GOAL (mandatory requirement)   │
                                  │ MANDATORY: The following user-set │
                                  │ goal condition MUST be            │
                                  │ demonstrably met.                 │
                                  │                                   │
                                  │ Evidence must be in the           │
                                  │ transcript already. Bare          │
                                  │ assertions do not count.          │
                                  └──────────────────────────────────┘
                                         │
                                         ▼
                                  Same two-LLM flow as Layer 2
                                  (self-assessment + judge)
                                         │
                                         ▼
                                  decideGoalTransition():
                                  ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
                                  │                                   │
                                  │  attempts >= 16?                  │
                                  │  OR now >= deadline?              │
                                  │     → status = "exhausted"        │
                                  │       stop, notify user           │
                                  │                                   │
                                  │  judge: complete = true?          │
                                  │     → status = "achieved"         │
                                  │       stop cleanly                │
                                  │                                   │
                                  │  otherwise:                       │
                                  │     → attempts += 1               │
                                  │       inject continuation prompt  │
                                  │       (budget only burns here,    │
                                  │        not on early-returns)      │
                                  └──────────────────────────────────┘
                                         │
                              ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                              │                     │
                         stop cleanly          agent wakes up,
                         (achieved or          works toward goal,
                          exhausted)           session.idle fires
                                               again, loop repeats
```

What counts as evidence vs. what does not:

```
✓  Evidence (transcript shows it happened):
   - Tool output: bun test → exit 0, 42 passed
   - gh pr create output with PR number
   - CI URL + "All checks passed"

✗  Not evidence (bare assertion):
   - "The tests should pass now"
   - "I've implemented what was needed"
   - "This satisfies the goal"
```

---

## How the Three Layers Relate

```
User sends a prompt
        │
        ▼
  Agent mode?
        │
        ├── autopilot ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │                                                                │
        │   prompt.ts loop handles everything internally:                │
        │   agent stops without tool call → inject reminder → continue   │
        │   agent calls autopilot_exit() → inject summary → go idle      │
        │                                                                │
        │   session.idle fires ONCE at the very end.                     │
        │   Supervisor sees it but the agent is already done.            │
        │                                                                │
        └── build ────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
                                                                      │ │
            Agent finishes a turn normally → session.idle fires       │ │
                    │                                                  │ │
                    ▼                                                  │ │
            Supervisor plugin runs (Layer 2)                          │ │
                    │                                                  │ │
                    ├── complete → stay idle                           │ │
                    │                                                  │ │
                    └── not complete:                                  │ │
                           Is a /goal active? (Layer 3)               │ │
                                  │                                   │ │
                             Yes  │  No                               │ │
                                  │   └── pattern nudge (≤ 3 tries)   │ │
                                  │                                   │ │
                                  └── goal-locked nudge (≤ 16 tries,  │ │
                                       evidence required)             │ │
                                         │                            │ │
                                         ▼                            │ │
                                    inject prompt ────────────────────┘ │
                                    agent wakes up,                      │
                                    continues work,                      │
                                    session.idle fires again             │
                                    (loop back up)                       │
                                                                         │
                                                               ◄─────────┘
                                                         autopilot ends here:
                                                         final idle, done
```

**Summary:**

| Layer | Trigger | Mechanism | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| Autopilot | agent stops without tool call | inject `<system-reminder>` inside prompt loop | 50 reflections |
| Supervisor | `session.idle` | two-LLM judge, pattern matching | 3 nudges |
| `/goal` | `session.idle` + active goal | same judge + evidence-backed criterion | 16 nudges, 30 min |

Use autopilot when the task is well-scoped and you want to hand off completely.  
Use the supervisor (always-on by default) for everyday build mode.  
Use `/goal` when you have an objective acceptance criterion.

---


<details>
<summary>67 lines</summary>

```
---
name: blogpost
description: "Write or edit blog posts in the user's preferred style: short, technical, software-engineer voice, understandable to non-experts, value-first, with light SEO and subtle promotion of the relevant product or service. Use when asked to create, draft, rewrite, review, or improve a blog post, article, technical announcement, founder note, or SEO content."
---

# Blogpost

Write like a software engineer explaining useful work to a smart non-expert.

## Voice

- Short.
- Technical, plain.
- No motivational filler, no corporate tone.
- Mom-understandable point.
- Concrete examples over abstract claims.
- Say: what was built, why it matters, how to use it.
- First-person plural where natural: `we built`, `we learned`, `we use`.

## Goal

Value to the reader first. Good value:
- Practical setup people can copy
- Failure mode people can avoid
- Design trade-off explained clearly
- Cost or operational lesson
- Small product insight from real work

Promotion is secondary. Mention product briefly where context helps.

## Structure

1. Concrete problem
2. Simple idea
3. Working approach
4. Trade-offs / options
5. Recommended path
6. Short product/service connection

Short sections. Headings that say the point directly.

## Style rules

- Ban: `In today's world`, `game changer`, `seamless`, `robust`, `unlock`, `revolutionize`, SaaS filler.
- No long intros. No nested bullets. No fake certainty (`for now`, `for this case`, `the MVP is` instead).
- Code snippets only when useful.
- Tables only for clear comparisons.
- Define acronyms on first use if a normal reader may not know them.

## SEO

Quietly:
- Main keyword in title if natural
- One clear meta description / excerpt
- Related terms in prose, not stuffing
- Search-friendly phrases people actually type

Examples:
- `Chrome DevTools remote browser control`
- `cloud-hosted OpenClaw local Chrome`
- `Tailscale reverse tunnel Chrome DevTools`

## Product mention

Light.

Good:
- `This is why OpenClaw keeps the browser-control path simple: local when it should be local, cloud when the workload belongs in the cloud.`
- `OpenClaw uses this pattern so a cloud agent can work with the browser context the user already has.`

Bad:
- `Sign up now and transform your workflow.`
- `OpenClaw is the ultimate revolutionary browser automation platform.`

## Editing existing

- Preserve author POV
- Cut filler before adding text
- Tighten titles/subtitles
- Verify post teaches something useful
- Keep promo subtle and near the end unless explicit launch post

## Final check

- Reader learns something practical
- Main point clear in first 3 paragraphs
- Non-specialist understandable
- Product mention present but not dominant
- No unnecessary complexity
<shellId: 84 completed with exit code 0>
```

</details>

<sub>⏱️ 579m 44s</sub>

### 💭 Reasoning

*Now I understand the exact code. The autopilot reflection logic is at lines 1448-1471 in `prompt.ts`. The fix would be to replace the generic `PROMPT_AUTOPILOT` nudge with a judge-based evaluation.

Here's what the fix should do:
1. Instead of just injecting a generic "keep going" message, run a quick judge evaluation
2. If the judge says the task is done → allow break (or require `autopilot_exit` to be called)
3. If the judge says not done → inject targeted "what's missing" feedback

However, this is complex because:


- The judge currently depends on the supervisor plugin, which is external and not part of core opencode
- I'd need to either inline a judge or call out to the supervisor plugin, but that uses the opencode API client rather than the Effect service layer
- A simpler approach would be to run a lightweight self-evaluation using the same model against the message history with a dedicated judge prompt, then parse whether the task is complete and what's missing
- If complete, allow exit; if not, inject targeted feedback instead of generic nudges

The better strategy is to replace the generic autopilot reminder with a structured self-evaluation prompt that the agent runs inline—no separate session needed. I'll create a judge prompt that forces the agent to assess task completion, identify gaps, and outline concrete next steps.

The key distinction is where the judge fires: it should intercept the autopilot_exit call itself, not just nudge during reflection. So when the agent tries to exit, I run a quick evaluation—if the judge says the task isn't done, reject the exit and provide specific feedback about what's missing; if it's done, allow it through.

Implementing this means adding the evaluation logic directly into the autopilot_exit tool, checking the session state before completing... this task fully done? What is the single next action I'll take? I should only call autopilot_exit when there's nothing left to complete.

For the exit guard, I'm thinking about checking the last assistant message for language that suggests incomplete work — phrases like "next steps" or "to complete this" — and reject the exit if I find them. But a truly robust check would need its own LLM call. Let me see what services are available in the Effect layer to make that happen. I'm looking at what's imported in autopilot.ts — Session, Provider, Database — to figure out if I can call the model from within the tool itself.

Actually, I think a pragmatic two-part approach makes more sense: first, improve the nudge prompt in autopilot.txt with a structured self-assessment format instead of generic language, and second, add validation to autopilot_exit that checks whether the agent has actually completed the work before allowing an exit.

For the exit validation, I could use the Provider service to make an LLM call that judges whether the last message contains completion signals or pending work indicators. But given the complexity and time constraints, I'm going to take a simpler route: update the blog post with an honest assessment, implement the structured nudge prompt as the core fix, and file a GitHub issue for the full inline judge as a follow-up. The structured prompt alone will force the agent to explicitly enumerate what's still missing.*
